# Named addresses in Lords2.exe

GOG Windows build, 21 Jul 2009, 1,031,680 bytes. `ImageBase = 0x400000`, **no ASLR**, so
every address below is stable across runs and safe to hardcode.

Ghidra project: `E:\dev\ghidra-projects\lords2`. The binary has no symbols; these names
are ours. Where a name is a guess, the confidence column says so.

## The names live in the database too

**[`symbols.json`](symbols.json) is the single source of truth.** The tables in this file
are generated from it, and the same file drives a Ghidra script that pushes the names,
signatures and plate comments into the project, so anyone opening `Lords2.exe` in Ghidra
sees `Merchant_SpawnAll`, not `FUN_00427ed0`.

```powershell
# apply everything in docs/symbols.json to the Ghidra database (idempotent; --dry-run to preview)
$env:JAVA_HOME = "C:\Program Files\Microsoft\jdk-21.0.12.101-hotspot"
& "E:\dev\tools\ghidra_12.1.3_PUBLIC\support\analyzeHeadless.bat" `
    "E:\dev\ghidra-projects" lords2 -process Lords2.exe -noanalysis `
    -scriptPath "E:\dev\lords2\ghidra_scripts" -postScript ApplySymbols.java
```

```bash
node tools/symbols/symbols_md.js           # regenerate the tables below from symbols.json
node tools/symbols/symbols_md.js --check   # fail if this file is out of date
```

**Adding a name is a one-line edit in `symbols.json`**, then those two commands. Do not
hand-edit the tables between the `BEGIN`/`END` markers — `--check` will catch it. Prose
outside the markers is yours to write.

## Record *fields* live in `records.json`, not here

A name in `symbols.json` names **one address**. That is the wrong shape for a record array:
`g_counties` labels `0x0053F9B0` and nothing labels `county[i].happiness`, so the decompiler
used to invent a synthetic global for every field of every one of the five stride-indexed
arrays — 334 of them, a tenth of the globals in the corpus.

[`records.json`](records.json) holds those layouts instead, and
`ghidra_scripts/ApplyRecords.java` turns them into Ghidra structs so the decompiler writes
`g_counties[i].happiness`. `tools/oracle/decompile-all.ps1` runs it after `ApplySymbols`, so
a field added to the JSON reaches the whole corpus on the next rebuild.

```powershell
& "E:\dev\tools\ghidra_12.1.3_PUBLIC\support\analyzeHeadless.bat" `
    "E:\dev\ghidra-projects" lords2 -process Lords2.exe -noanalysis `
    -scriptPath "E:\dev\lords2\ghidra_scripts" -postScript ApplyRecords.java --dry-run
```

**A field goes in only when its width is measured.** `ghidra_scripts/RecordProbe.java`
reports, for every offset in every record array, the p-code `LOAD`/`STORE` width the binary
actually uses there. Documented meaning plus disagreeing width is a gap, not a field — a
wrong field name propagates into every function that touches it, which is correction C3 in
`docs/decisions.md`. `docs/method.md` §7.5 has the before-and-after.

---

## Sprite rendering

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: sprite -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x0040A21A` | `Pl8_DrawFrame(buf, frame, x, y)` | verified | Reads the frame record at buf + frame*0x10 + 8, range-checks the data offset, calls the clipper, dispatches to a blitter. Independently confirms the 8-byte header / 16-byte record layout inferred from offset arithmetic. Also reads record bytes +12, +13 and +14. |
| `0x0040464C` | `Clip_Horizontal(left, right)` | verified | Computes horizontal clipping and sets g_clipState, g_clipVisibleWidth, g_clipSrcSkip, g_clipDstAdvance. |
| `0x0040477B` | `Clip_Vertical(top, bottom)` | verified | Sets g_blitRowCount to height - rowsClippedAtTop, unconditionally. That is why the byte-0x0D load in Pl8_DrawFrame is dead. |
| `0x004B43B1` | `Blit_Unclipped(buf)` | verified | Copies g_spriteWidth bytes per row, skipping zero bytes; 4x unrolled. Loops g_blitRowCount rows - the clipped row count, not the frame height. Palette index 0 is transparent: this is an engine property, not something in the file format. |
| `0x004B446D` | `Blit_ClippedLeft(buf)` | inferred | Selected when g_clipState is 1. |
| `0x004B44D2` | `Blit_ClippedRight(buf)` | verified | As Blit_Unclipped, but also advances the source pointer by the clipped-off remainder each row. |
| `0x00402A14` | `Glyph_Draw(font, ch)` | verified | Text blitter. Shifts the destination down by frame-record byte 0x0D before clipping, reserving rows above the rectangle. The only consumer of that byte. |

**Globals**

| Address | Name | Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x005CDD20` | `g_clipState` | verified | Clip state: 0 unclipped, 1 clipped left, 2 clipped right, 5 fully offscreen. |
| `0x00591514` | `g_clipVisibleWidth` | verified | Visible (clipped) sprite width. |
| `0x0059150C` | `g_clipSrcSkip` | verified | Source bytes skipped per row = width - visible width. |
| `0x005CD400` | `g_clipDstAdvance` | verified | Destination advance per row = screen stride - visible width. |
| `0x005C9258` | `g_spriteWidth` | verified | Current sprite width. |
| `0x005AEB70` | `g_spriteHeight` | verified | Current sprite height. |
| `0x0058FE04` | `g_spriteDataOffset` | verified | Current source data offset within the PL8 buffer. |
| `0x004EB274` | `g_screenStride` | inferred | Screen stride, 640. |
| `0x005BB478` | `g_blitRowCount` | verified | Blitter row counter. Not frame-record byte 0x0D - Clip_Vertical overwrites it before every blit, so the byte-0x0D load in Pl8_DrawFrame is dead. |
| `0x004EABD0` | `g_ddrawInterface` | verified | A DirectDraw interface pointer. It was NULL at the crash at 0x004522B5, which happened when the window was deactivated during startup under DxWnd. |
| `0x0053E918` | `g_villani1Sheet` | verified | villani1.pl8, loaded by Village_Draw. Read by ONE blit in the whole executable - Village_Animate's iron-mine overlay, Pl8_DrawFrame(this, DAT_004D2938, 0xA4, top + 0xC). The file holds 21 frames of 40x62 and the counter wraps at 0x11, so three of them are never drawn. |
| `0x005533C4` | `g_villani2Sheet` | verified | villani2.pl8, loaded by Village_Draw. 44 frames: five equal-sized animation blocks (0-6 at 26x29, 7-14 at 39x40, 15-24 at 15x12, 25-32 at 32x42, 33-39 at 19x18) that are exactly the five overlays Village_Animate draws from it, then the three static buildings Village_Draw blits - 0x28 stone, 0x29 wood, 0x2B iron - and a 2x2 stub at 42. |

<!-- END symbols.json: sprite -->

**Palette index 0 is transparent.** Every blitter copies a byte only when it is non-zero.
This is a property of the engine, not something recorded in the file format — and it is
the bug our own decoder had until the binary revealed it.

A further family of blitters sits around `0x004BC000`–`0x004BF000`. They also read the
clip state and are presumably variants for other pixel paths. Not yet examined.

## Text (`L2.eng`)

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: text -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x0040187A` | `Eng_GroupBase(group)` | verified | Returns the byte offset of an L2.eng group, read as the low 24 bits of the u32 at file offset 8 + group*4. See docs/formats/eng.md. |
| `0x00402D37` | `Eng_DrawString(group, index, x, y, font, colour)` | verified | Draws string `index` of L2.eng group `group`: Eng_GroupBase(group), then skip `index` NUL-terminated strings, then skip any bytes below 0x20. The (group, index) pairs in the callers are the best evidence available for what a subsystem is. |
| `0x004018D7` | `Eng_Seek(group, index)` | verified | Walks L2.eng to the (group, index) string and leaves the pointer in g_engCursor, exactly as docs/formats/eng.md 1.3 describes. New-game setup calls Eng_Seek(7, realm[+0x07]) and copies 16 bytes into g_playerNames - the game naming its own lords out of group 7, which is what fixes 1 = The Knight, 2 = The Baron, 3 = The Countess, 4 = The Bishop. |
| `0x004017BF` | `Eng_CopyString(dest, group, index, len)` | verified | Copies len bytes of L2.eng string (group, index) into a caller buffer, walking the group exactly as Eng_DrawString does. The fifth and last function that takes a literal (group, index) pair - the others are Eng_DrawString, Eng_Seek, Ui_DrawCentred and FUN_0040328E - and therefore the fifth place a call site names its own subject. Note the pair is at arguments 2 and 3 here, not 1 and 2. |

**Globals**

| Address | Name | Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x005C9270` | `g_engCursor` | verified | Where Eng_Seek leaves its pointer; the caller reads or copies the string from here. |
| `0x0058FE9C` | `g_embossGrey` | verified | When non-zero, Ui_DrawText embosses with 0x3F above and 0x26 below instead of the screen-id pair 0x10/0x1F - black over a light grey rather than a dark olive over parchment yellow. Set by exactly three functions and cleared immediately after each: CountyStrip_Draw around the three Sovereign land of ... lines, Screen_Armoury, and FUN_004180F6. It is the reason the county name and the banner beneath it are embossed differently on the same plate; docs/bugs.md B64. |
| `0x005AEB90` | `g_dropShadow` | verified | When non-zero, Ui_DrawText draws a DROP SHADOW rather than an emboss: one pass at (x+1, y+1) in colour 0x3F, then the glyph at (x, y). Set by the county strip's seven produce-row painters and by CountyStrip_DrawCastleIcon. g_flatText (DAT_005AEA40) is tested first and wins. |

<!-- END symbols.json: text -->

`Eng_DrawString`'s `(group, index)` arguments are the single most useful naming tool in the
binary: find the call, look the string up in `L2.eng`, and the surrounding code names
itself. That is how unit type 3 was identified as the merchant — see
[`formats/plane4.md`](formats/plane4.md) §3.

Reads arm_cros\|mace\|swor\|pike\|bow\|mail.pl8 by g_armourySelectedType (falling through to arm_cros.pl8), then Ui_DrawBox(0x60, 4, 0x1A, 7), Ui_OkButton(0x1E4, 0x58, 0), Ui_DrawInsetRect(0x65, 9, 0x66, 0x66) and frame 0 of the weapon's 24-frame 100x100 animation at (0x66, 10), then FUN_00418E2D for the count and the spare. [V] |

## Map loading and rendering

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: map -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004676E0` | `Map_InitScenario()` | verified | Whole map bring-up for scenario g_scenarioIndex: Map_LoadPlanes, Map_LoadLattice, Minimap_Load, Map_PlaceStartingFields, then Merchant_PickStartCounties. |
| `0x00467770` | `Map_LoadPlanes(slot)` | verified | Reads the six 64x64 byte planes of a map slot, at slot offsets +0x0000 ... +0x5000, in a 64x64 nest (y outer, x inner). Tracks the highest county id below 0x11 into g_countyCount, and dispatches plane 4 to Merchant_RouteAppend for a plane-0 bit 0x40 tile (the county town) or PlayerStart_Record for a bit 0x80 tile (the castle). This description previously had the two the other way round; see decisions.md C25. Under the corrected reading a merchant route is a list of market towns and a player start is a castle, which is what both are. See docs/formats/maps.md. |
| `0x0046797D` | `Map_LoadLattice(slot)` | verified | Reads the trailing 65x129 layer - for(row < 0x81) for(col < 0x41) - storing byte + 0x0FFF0000 into a dword array of row stride 0x104. |
| `0x0046A037` | `Minimap_Load(slot)` | verified | Loads the two 128x128 minimap rasters for map slot 0..59 out of MAPnn.PL8: name = "map01.pl8" + (slot>>2)*0x10, frame (slot&3)*5 -> g_minimapCounty (county id per pixel) and frame (slot&3)*5+1 -> g_minimapPixels (the picture). Each MAPnn.PL8 therefore carries four slots, and the 11 shipped files cover exactly the 44 used slots. It was named Map_LoadTileSets and described as picking a season variant; it does neither - the tile sets are loaded by Gfx_LoadCountyMode and the season is a separate global. |
| `0x00468C41` | `County_FindDwellingPlots(county)` | verified | Sweeps the 64x64 grid for this county's tiles with plane-0 bit 0x10 set and records each one's byte offset in county.dwellingPlots, copying g_tiles[t].frame into .savedFrame and zeroing .content. Called once per county from Counties_PlaceSites, immediately after County_FindTownTile. [V] - the check that could have failed is arithmetic: the destination is four i32 slots at county +0x80 with no bound on the counter, and it ends exactly on fieldProgress at +0x90, so a county with five 0x10 tiles would corrupt a field. maps-layers.md 2.3 measured exactly four per county over all 434 counties of the 44 shipped maps. |
| `0x004684C6` | `County_UpdateDwellings(county, wanted)` | verified | Raises or clears a dwelling on each of the four plots County_FindDwellingPlots found. Population_UpdateAll calls it once per county per season with wanted banded on population: 0 below 601, 1 below 1001, 2 below 1401, 3 below 1601, else 4. Each of the first `wanted` plots steps g_tiles[t].content one size up the ladder 0x13 -> 0x12 -> 0x11 -> 0x10 (frame 0x3C for the two small sizes, 0x3B for the two large), sets the bank to Town (0x0C) with bit 0x80 and sets flags bit 0x10; each plot beyond `wanted` is razed - frame restored from savedFrame, bank cleared back to base, flags bit 0x10 cleared, content 0. This answers maps-layers.md 8's open question about the four 0x10 plots. [V] - the razing branch reads savedFrame, the byte maps-layers.md 5.3 predicted exists for exactly this and for nothing else; had +0x80 not been the plot list it would restore an unrelated tile's terrain. Unit_BurnDwelling is the inverse: it drives 0x10 back to 0x13 on one plot. |
| `0x00467A36` | `Map_PlaceStartingFields()` | verified | Gives every farm tile its starting crop. Sweeps the 64x64 grid and, for each tile with a county in 1..17 and plane0 bit 0x20 set, counts it into county +0x205 and writes g_tiles[t].content and .frame by DIFFICULTY and by the tile's ordinal within its county: easiest, first 8 pasture (content 0x14, frame base 104) then fallow (content 1, base 84); difficulty 1, 6 pasture then 2 fallow then wild (content 0, base 80); difficulty 2, 4 then 2 then wild; hardest, 4 pasture then wild. The frame is base + ((stored frame + 0xB0) & 3), a four-variant pick, and the three bases are the crop states maps-layers.md 2.4 reads bit 0x20 as farmland from. It was called Map_PlaceDwellings: it places no dwelling, and the four dwelling plots per county are County_FindDwellingPlots and County_UpdateDwellings. See decisions.md C29. |
| `0x0040526E` | `Map_RenderIso()` | verified | Walks the 65x129 lattice. Cells below 0x0FFF0000 hold a runtime offset into the tile array; cells still holding 0x0FFF0000 + b are off-map surround, where b is the background tile graphic index from the file. |
| `0x004063C1` | `Map_DrawTile(x, y, tileOffset)` | verified | Draws one map tile. bank = tile[+2] & 0x1c selects one of five sprite tables; tile[+3] is the frame index within it; tile[+1] is the flags and tile[+7] the county. |
| `0x00429800` | `Map_LatticeCellForTile(tileOffset)` | verified | Reverse lookup: scans the screen lattice for a given tile-array offset to find its screen cell. |
| `0x0046D7F4` | `Terrain_Set(tileByteOffset, terrain, variant)` | verified | THE SINGLE WRITER of a tile's content byte, and it picks the tile's picture in the same breath - so this one function is the terrain -> frame mapping a renderer needs. Sixteen call sites: Field_SetType (the brush), Field_ReclaimTick, Field_OrderReclamation, County_DestroyField, Grain_SeasonTick and Herd_UpdateCrowding through FUN_00469D21, Map_PlaceStartingFields, and nine helpers around 0x00469xxx. frame = ((frame - oldBase) & 3) + base + variant*4; bank = (((bank \| 1) & 0xE3) \| layer) & 0x7F, then \|= 0x80 for terrain 0x0F..0x16. Base ladder on the NEW terrain, in the order the code tests it: 0 -> 80 roads, 0x17 -> 130 BASE, 0x18 -> 134 BASE, 1 -> 84, 0x19/0x1A/0x1B/0x1C -> 108/112/116/120, terrain < 0x13 -> 88, everything else -> 104 (a bare else, so 0x1D and up land there too - maps-layers.md 5.5's table reads as if 0x13..0x16 were exhaustive). oldBase is 0x82 when the PREVIOUS terrain was 0x17 or 0x18 and 0 otherwise; every other base is a multiple of 4, so the `& 3` preserves the tile's variant forever and the correction exists only for the two bases that are not. Consequence: the low two bits of a farm tile's frame never change, so the picture is a pure function of the terrain and the frame the map file stored. The third parameter is DEAD - all sixteen call sites pass 0, including both callers of the one function that forwards it. Named here after four documents referred to it only as FUN_0046D7F4; docs/formats/maps-layers.md 5.5 is the write-up and l2_view::campaign::field_graphic is the reimplementation. |
| `0x0046733C` | `Map_FindFreeTileNear(x, y)` | verified | For r = 1..5, Map_FindFreeRoadTileNear(x, y, r + 1) then Map_FindFreeOpenTileNear(x, y, r) - so at every radius a road one ring wider beats open ground one ring narrower. Leaves the answer in g_foundTileX / g_foundTileY and returns the radius, or 0. Its one caller is Army_Split. |
| `0x0049BC5F` | `PlayerStart_Compact()` | verified | Drops the player-start slots above the number of live realms by bubbling the table down, so a game with fewer lords than the map seats leaves the extra start counties unowned rather than off the end of the array. |
| `0x00467CA6` | `County_BuildNeighbours()` | verified | Builds every county zero-terminated adjacency list at county +0x5C and its count at +0x5B, once, from Map_InitScenario. Sweeps the 64x64 grid y outer x inner and, for each tile of a county in 1..17, appends each of its four 4-adjacent tiles other county to the list if it is not already there, stopping at sixteen entries. [V] The order is the scan order and not a sort, which matters because the list is walked by index in half a dozen places. Reproduced in l2_scenario::newgame::adjacency, whose output equals the England turn-one save stored lists for all fourteen counties - two files authored separately. |
| `0x0046DA4B` | `County_CollectFieldTiles(clearProgress)` | verified | Fills g_countyFieldTiles - 17 rows of twenty i32 byte offsets - from the map, at the end of Map_InitScenario, and clears the twenty u16 at county +0x90 when the argument is 1. Sweeps tiles in ascending byte-offset order and puts each farmland tile (plane-0 bit 0x20) of a county in 1..17 in that county first free slot. [V] AND THE TWENTY-SLOT TABLE RAZES WHAT WILL NOT FIT: a county twenty-first farm tile takes content 0, frame 6, flags 0 and its bank cleared back to base - it stops being farmland at all, before the first season runs. So twenty fields per county is a fact about the map rather than a cap on a counter. No shipped map exercises the razing branch; crates/l2-scenario/tests/newgame.rs exercises it on a synthetic slot. See decisions.md C62. |
| `0x004A6735` | `Ai_FindTownTile(unit, county)` | verified | The tile of the county carrying plane-0 0x40 - the county TOWN, not the castle - nearest by Manhattan distance to the unit, into g_foundTileX/Y; falls back to the county anchor at +0x6C/+0x6D. Distinct from County_FindTownTile (0x00467FD1), which finds the same bit once at map load to place that anchor. Stepping onto the result runs Army_AttackCounty. Ties go to the lowest tile offset, and offset 0 doubles as not-found, so tile (0,0) can never be returned. |
| `0x004A65A3` | `Ai_FindCastleBuildingTile(unit, county)` | verified | The tile of the county carrying plane-0 0x80 with terrain 0x15..0x19 - the five BUILT castle types, excluding the 0x14 plot - nearest by Manhattan distance to the unit; falls back to the stored castle tile at county +0x74/+0x75. Stepping onto the result runs Unit_ReachCastleBuilding: Army_Garrison when the county is the mover own, Army_BeginSiege when it is not. |
| `0x004A689D` | `Ai_FindStandingCropTile(unit, county)` | verified | The tile of the county carrying plane-0 0x20 with terrain 3..22 - a standing crop or a pasture, excluding wild and fallow - nearest by Manhattan distance to the unit; falls back to the county anchor. This is what the AI raiding party (mission 7) is aimed at, which is why a raid damages the harvest. |
| `0x00467EB0` | `County_BordersRealm(realm, county)` | verified | Walks the county zero-terminated 16-slot adjacency list at +0x5C and returns 1 if any neighbour is owned by that realm. It is the adjacency gate on every county the AI picks for its main army, which is why an AI attack always lands next to its own territory - and why a raid, whose chooser skips this test, need not. |

**Globals**

| Address | Name | Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004DA050` | `g_resourceTable` | verified | The game's resource directory: {char name[16]; u32 size;} records. Entries 0-31 are the map tile sets, eight layers x four seasons; 32-63 the zoom-2 sets; 64+ the battle-map sets. See docs/formats/maps-layers.md 1.1. |
| `0x0053F034` | `g_scenarioIndex` | verified | The map slot, 0..59, used unshifted. Map_LoadLattice seeks slot * 0x80C1 (the whole slot stride), Eng_DrawString(101, g_scenarioIndex) indexes the 60 slot names in L2.eng group 101, and Minimap_Load takes the MAPnn.PL8 file from slot >> 2 and one of its four slots from slot & 3. An earlier note here said the low 2 bits select the season variant of the tile set: they do not - Gfx_LoadCountyMode takes the season from g_season. |
| `0x00522F90` | `g_tiles` | verified | Runtime tile array, 4096 records x 8 bytes in a 64x64 grid. Typed as struct Tile in docs/records.json - all eight bytes named, and every one of the 441 accesses in the binary is one byte wide (RecordProbe). The code indexes it by BYTE OFFSET, (y*64 + x)*8, so a step east is +8 and a step south is +0x200; Ghidra renders the two idioms as g_tiles[y*0x40 + x].county and (&g_tiles[0].county)[tileOffset]. See docs/formats/maps-layers.md 5.3. |
| `0x0055CEA0` | `g_screenLattice` | verified | Runtime 129x65 dword screen lattice, row stride 0x104. Covered cells hold tileIndex*8; uncovered cells keep 0x0FFF0000 + backgroundFrame. |
| `0x0056D5DC` | `g_countyCount` | verified | Highest county id on the loaded map, i.e. the county count. Set by Map_LoadPlanes as max(plane5) over ids below 0x11. |

<!-- END symbols.json: map -->

Map slots are addressed by `File_ReadChunk` with `lseek(index * 0x80C1)` — 0x80C1 is
32,961, the slot stride. See [`formats/maps.md`](formats/maps.md) and
[`formats/maps-layers.md`](formats/maps-layers.md).

**`mapl2.exe` is a dead end.** Despite shipping in the game folder, its strings identify
it as the "L2 Battlemap editor" for `.skr` battle scenarios, and it contains no reference
to `l2_maps.dat` at all. The reference implementation for the campaign map format is
`Lords2.exe` itself. Recorded here so nobody re-investigates it.

## Units, merchants and counties

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: units -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x0046E1B0` | `Unit_Spawn(type, x, y, owner)` | verified | Allocates the first free slot of the 150-entry unit array g_units (stride 0x1A4) and places it at tile (x, y). Requires the tile to hold no unit and to have plane-0 bits 0xFC clear. Writes the unit index into byte +5 of the runtime tile record and sets bit 0x01 of byte +2. Types seen: 1 = army, 2 = revolting peasants, 3 = merchant, 4 = transport. |
| `0x0046CFBD` | `Map_FindFreeRoadTileNear(x, y, r)` | verified | Scans the (2r+1)^2 box around (x, y), clipped to the map, for a tile with no unit on it and plane-0 bit 0x01 (road) set. Returns 1 and leaves the tile in g_foundTileX / g_foundTileY. |
| `0x0046D130` | `Map_FindFreeOpenTileNear(x, y, r)` | verified | As Map_FindFreeRoadTileNear but accepts plane0 & 0xFD == 0 - plain grass, with or without the county-boundary bit. The fallback when no free road tile exists. |
| `0x00428007` | `County_FindFreeRoadTile(county)` | verified | Map_FindFreeRoadTileNear around the county's anchor tile for r = 1, 2, 3. |
| `0x00428078` | `County_FindFreeOpenTile(county)` | verified | Map_FindFreeOpenTileNear around the county's anchor tile for r = 1, 2, 3. |
| `0x00429153` | `Merchant_RouteAppend(county, route)` | verified | Appends `county` to the first free cell of row `route - 1` of g_merchantRoutes. Called from Map_LoadPlanes for every castle tile with a non-zero plane 4. See docs/formats/plane4.md. |
| `0x004290D0` | `Merchant_ResetRoutes()` | verified | Zeroes the 6x16 g_merchantRoutes table and the 6-byte g_merchantStartCounty array, at the top of Map_LoadPlanes. |
| `0x004291B3` | `Merchant_PickStartCounties()` | verified | For each of the six routes, picks a start county no earlier route has claimed: entry 0, then entries 2, 4, 6..., then 3, 5, 7... after running off the end; entry 1 is never tried and it gives up after five retries. Because g_merchantStartCounty starts zeroed, a row with no free candidate stores 0 - which stops Merchant_SpawnAll dead. On slot 15 (Rorschach) the dedup fails and two merchants share county 8. |
| `0x0042925E` | `Merchant_StartCountyTaken(county)` | verified | 1 if `county` already appears in g_merchantStartCounty. Note it also returns 1 for county 0, because unset entries are 0. |
| `0x00427ED0` | `Merchant_SpawnAll()` | verified | Spawns one type-3 merchant, owner 6 (nobody), on a free road tile near each start county, stopping at the first zero start county. Sets the unit's route index (its name, L2.eng group 5) and route cursor, and counts them into g_merchantCount. 18 of the 44 shipped maps get all six merchants; one gets a single merchant. |
| `0x004280E9` | `Merchant_AdvanceAll()` | verified | Turn phase 6, step 1. For every type-3 unit awaiting a destination, walks row (unitIndex - 1) of g_merchantRoutes cyclically from the unit's route cursor, skipping entries above g_countyCount, and sends the merchant to the next county on its route. It indexes the table by unit index, not by the unit's stored route number - which only works because the merchants occupy unit slots 1..6. |
| `0x0049BBE8` | `PlayerStart_Record(county, slot)` | verified | Writes {county, slot} into g_playerStartTable[slot] and counts non-zero slots into g_playerStartCount. | `0x0041B19D` | `UnitPanel_Draw()` | verified | Draws the info panel for the selected unit. Its Eng_DrawString (group, index) pairs name every unit type: L2.eng group 31 strings 0 'Merchant.', 5 'Revolting peasants.', 2 'Supplies for', 15/16 army; descriptions 12/13/14/15/16. For a merchant it also draws group 5 indexed by the route number - the merchant's name. |
| `0x004296B5` | `Transport_Deliver(unit, county)` | verified | If the type-4 unit's destination county matches, adds its cargo to that county's stores. |
| `0x004292AF` | `Transport_Spawn(owner, county, dest)` | inferred | Creates a type-4 transport near `county` and deducts the cargo from that county's stores. |
| `0x004658C1` | `Unit_EnterOccupiedTile()` | inferred | Resolves what happens when the moving unit enters a tile already holding one. Returns immediately for types 3 and 4, so merchants and transports are never attacked; an enemy transport is captured instead. |
| `0x0046873F` | `Unit_TrampleTile(unit, tileOffset)` | inferred | Damages a crop tile an army passes through in a county it does not own. Begins `if (type != 3 && type != 4)` - merchants and transports do no damage. |
| `0x00468AE2` | `Unit_BurnDwelling(unit, tileOffset)` | inferred | Burns a dwelling an army passes through in a county it does not own, and cuts that county's population. Same type-3/type-4 exemption. |

| `0x004651B9` | `Units_ResetMoves()` | verified | Start of season: clears moveState (+0x14C) and movesUsed (+0x153) on all 150 unit slots. Called from turn phase 7. |
| `0x0046521F` | `Army_Tick()` | verified | The type-1 (army) tick. Writes moveAllowance (+0x154) = 15 unconditionally, picks the sprite bank by men (<301 -> 0x48, <601 -> 0x60, else 0x78) plus 3*facing plus the walk frame, calls Unit_EnterCounty when the tile county differs from +0x10, and steps the mover. |
| `0x00465D28` | `Unit_Step()` | verified | Walks one unit along its stored path. Stops when movesUsed reaches moveAllowance. Dispatches Transport_Deliver, Unit_TrampleTile and Unit_BurnDwelling from Unit_TryEnterTile's return code. |
| `0x0046634D` | `Unit_StepOnce(dir)` | verified | One animation tick of a move: accumulates +0x149 to 16, then calls Unit_NeighbourTile and charges movesUsed +1 on a road (+0x14D set) or +3 otherwise. |
| `0x00466893` | `Unit_NeighbourTile(dir)` | verified | Maps a direction 0..7 to the neighbouring tile offset with edge-of-map guards and hands it to Unit_TryEnterTile. |
| `0x00466C3C` | `Unit_TryEnterTile(tileOffset)` | verified | Classifies the target tile and returns a code: occupied -> Unit_EnterOccupiedTile; road (plane0 0x01) -> 3; county town (0x40) -> 5; castle (0x80) -> 6; dwelling plot (0x10) -> 7; farm field (0x20) -> 8; otherwise 1. The 0x40 and 0x80 labels were swapped here until decisions.md C25; the codes themselves are unchanged. The entry guard that clears bit 0x80 when the terrain is 0x14 is County_FindCastleTile's stamp, which is how a built castle stops blocking its owner. |
| `0x00466D84` | `Unit_MoveInFacing()` | inferred | Commits one step of g_movingUnit in the facing it has already been given: erases the sprite, plays the per-kind movement sound (army 0x0C, mob 5, merchant/transport 0x0B), adds the eight compass deltas to x, y and tileOffset (+-1 tile, +-8 and +-0x200 bytes of g_tiles), redraws, then sets onRoad from bit 0x01 of the tile's plane-0 flag byte. Unit_StepOnce is the caller: it charges the move (3 off-road, 1 on-road), writes facing, and calls this. [D] - the check that could have failed is that all eight cases are exactly the eight compass deltas and that tileOffset stays (y*64+x)*8 in every one of them; a case that disagreed would have shown up as a ninth delta. |
| `0x0046673C` | `Unit_CrossField()` | verified | A step onto a farm field: charges 3 extra moves and, when the tile's county is not the unit's own, worsens diplomacy by 10 (human owners only) and calls County_DestroyField. Total cost 6, which is what g_moveCost holds for a field. |
| `0x00469E5B` | `County_DestroyField(tileOffset)` | verified | Removes one field: a grain field takes crop * (100/fieldsSown) percent off county +0x244 and decrements +0x201; a pasture takes the same fraction off the herd and decrements +0x200. The tile is repainted bare. |
| `0x00469E0E` | `County_TileIsField(tileOffset)` | verified | True when the tile terrain is in 2..0x16, the range County_DestroyField will act on. |
| `0x0046FF43` | `Move_BuildCostMap()` | verified | Rebuilds the 64x64 i16 campaign movement cost map g_moveCost from the map planes: 1 road, 3 open ground, 6 a standing field, 100 castle/settlement/plot, 0 impassable. Run once a season from turn phase 7. |
| `0x0046F700` | `Move_FloodFill(who, x, y, mode)` | inferred | Fills the campaign distance field (g_moveDistLocal for the local player, g_moveDistOther otherwise) outward from a tile using g_moveCost. Assumed cost-weighted breadth-first; the body was not read. |
| `0x004707BE` | `Path_CopyToUnit(slot, unit)` | verified | Copies g_pathBuf[slot] into the unit record: length to +0x1C and up to 150 (x, y) pairs to +0x1D. 150 * 2 = 300 bytes, ending exactly at +0x149. |
| `0x004708D0` | `Path_ClearBuf(slot)` | verified | Zeroes one of the shared campaign path buffers and its length. |
| `0x004A98FE` | `Unit_OrderMove(who, unit, x, y, mode)` | verified | Gives a unit a destination: rebuild the cost map, flood-fill from the unit, extract the path, copy it into the record, set moveState = 2 and the destination x/y. |
| `0x004A91BA` | `Path_MarkPreviewTiles()` | verified | Sets bit 0x40 of byte +2 of every runtime tile record along the local player's path buffer. Map_DrawPathMarker draws the gold path balls on exactly those tiles. |
| `0x004A8E0B` | `Map_HoverUnitTarget()` | inferred | Runs the campaign pathfinder to the tile under the cursor and collects what a click there would do. When moveAllowance minus movesUsed is less than the path distance it zeroes every collected target, so an out-of-range click does nothing. |
| `0x004ABB36` | `Unit_EnterCounty(unit, county)` | verified | Called when a unit crosses a county border: sets +0x10, fires County_GreetArmy for a neutral county, and for an owned one sends message 0xAA (L2.eng group 170, the sixteen invasion taunts) when that county is the unit's declared destination. |
| `0x004ABF77` | `County_GreetArmy(unit, county)` | verified | Picks a neutral county's reaction to an army entering: happiness below 10 -> msg 0x82, below 30 -> 0x83, otherwise by the army as a percentage of the county population - 100 or more -> 0x83, 50..99 -> 0x84, 20..49 -> 0x85, under 20 -> 0x86 (L2.eng group 134, the border-violation message). The bigger the army the politer the reply. |
| `0x004AD6C0` | `Army_RecountCountyTroops()` | verified | Zeroes county +0x198 and +0x19C for all 16 counties, then adds every non-garrisoned type-1 or type-2 unit's men to the friendly or enemy total of the county it stands in (an ally, by realm +0x81, counts friendly), and re-runs Ration_Apply for every county. |
| `0x004ACE5E` | `Army_Starve()` | verified | Once a season from Wages_PayAll, and only when Army foraging is on. | `0x004AD16C` | `Army_Desert(unit)` | verified | Takes 10 percent off each of the seven troop counts (only where the count exceeds 10) and subtracts the same total from +0x168. Used by starvation and by bankruptcy stages 2..4. |
| `0x00435EBC` | `Levy_SetPercent(county, pct)` | verified | The raise-army slider: men = Pct(population, pct), happiness cost = g_armyHappinessCost[pct] plus county +0x2F4, clamped 0..100, then the percentage is walked back until county happiness minus the cost is at least 1. | `0x004AAA80` | `Levy_Init(county, men)` | verified | Zeroes the realm's 8-slot levy basket, seeds slots 1..6 with the realm's weapon stockpiles (realm +0x140 + (t-1)*4) and puts the levied headcount in slot 0 (unequipped peasants) and slot 7 (the total). |
| `0x004A9EB1` | `Levy_ConsumeWeapons(realm)` | verified | Subtracts each levy basket slot 1..6 from the realm's matching weapon stock, then re-runs the labour allocator. |
| `0x004A9F18` | `Levy_DebitPopulation(county)` | verified | Subtracts the levy total from county +0x24 (population) and +0x38 (the army line on the population panel). |
| `0x004A9A9A` | `Army_Create(realm, county, hireMercs, happinessCost)` | verified | Spawns a type-1 unit on a free road or open tile of the county and fills it from the levy basket: men to +0x168, slots 0..6 to +0x16C, the year to +0x164, the county's happiness to +0x166 as morale, a name from Army_PickName, optionally a mercenary band; then debits happiness and county +0x15 and sets the county levy surcharge +0x2F4 to 15. |
| `0x004A9F72` | `Army_PickName(realm)` | verified | Picks the least-used of the realm's 24 army-name slots at realm +0x2D and adds 2 to its counter. The names are L2.eng groups 94..98, 24 per lord. |
| `0x004AA039` | `Army_Destroy(unit)` | verified | Frees an army: releases its garrison or siege link, decrements its name-slot counter, clears the unit record and recomputes the realm's wage bill. |
| `0x004AA181` | `Army_Combine(into, from)` | verified | Merges one army into another when the combined men are under 1501 and at most one of them carries mercenaries (otherwise message 0xA7, L2.eng 167). It also migrates the siege link: the survivor inherits whichever of the two was besieging and the besieged garrison's +0x19A is repointed at it, which is what L2.eng 275 means by 'You must join with or dispose of the existing siegers.' The earlier 'keeps the lower movesUsed' here was wrong and was corrected in docs/armies.md section 2.7 without being corrected here. |

| `0x004AB383` | `Battle_ReturnToCampaign(resume)` | verified | Applies a battle result to the campaign. CORRECTED: g_battleLoser holds the WINNER, and this function is one of four sites that say so. The two branches are not mirror images. A wins: the county changes hands (County_ChangeOwner) if the loser was a garrison OR carried the +0x167 defence mark, the county's garrisonUnit is cleared, and the winner is charged +8 moves and then, if its owner is NOT human (+0x01 == 0 is the AI), set to moveAllowance - 1. B wins: the county NEVER changes hands - there is no County_ChangeOwner in that branch at all - only the attacker's garrison link is cleared, and only an AI winner pays, +7, while a winning human pays nothing. Either way the loser is destroyed and Diplo_Offend takes 20 off the loser's realm. ALSO CORRECTED: the 'except under autocalc, an army left with 50 or more men only has its siege lifted' rule this entry used to carry is wrong. Its gate is g_battleWithdrawal, which Battle_AutoResolve clears as its first statement; the flag is raised only by UnitOrder_SiegeAttKnight, so that path is a siege withdrawal and under autocalc the loser is always destroyed. See docs/decisions.md C31. |

| `0x004ACA2B` | `Mercenary_AdvanceAll()` | verified | Once a season: every unhired band clears its offer, counts its timer down and steps one county along, wrapping at g_countyCount; at zero it offers itself in that county and reloads. | `0x00418653` | `Screen_RaiseArmy()` | verified | The raise-army screen: L2.eng group 69 over the levy slider, the six weapon stocks from realm +0x140, the happiness cost, and the mercenary offer for county +0x1AD - count and nationality from g_mercBands, the hire price, and men/2 as the seasonal wage. |
| `0x00419354` | `Screen_SplitArmyRows()` | verified | The eight rows of the army-division screen (L2.eng group 17): troop types 0..6 from the levy basket in two columns, then row 7 as the mercenary band, then the two Total men lines. |
| `0x00421F14` | `Screen_SiegePrep()` | verified | The siege preparation screen (0x1D): L2.eng group 83, the seasons remaining from +0x19C, and a count plus a percent-complete bar for each of the three siege-engine records at +0x182, +0x188 and +0x18E. |
| `0x00468E61` | `County_PlaceResourceSites(county)` | verified | At load, scans the county for Town-bank tiles (runtime byte +2 masked with 0x1C equals 0x0C) and turns plane-2 descriptor 30, 0 and 20 into the iron, stone and wood industry sites: sets has_resource at +0x295 + c*0x18, stores the tile, and writes terrain 1, 4 and 10. This is where a county's resources come from - the map, not the county record. |
| `0x0046902A` | `County_PlaceBlacksmith(county)` | verified | Places the weapons industry site (record 2) on the county tile whose flags byte is exactly 0 - not a road, not a field, not a boundary - that is 4-adjacent to one of the county own farm fields and nearest the town anchor, ties going to the first in row-major order. It also ADDS plane-0 bit 0x80 to a tile the file gave no flags at all, which with the razed fields is one of only two places the runtime flags plane is not the file. [V] The blacksmith is derived rather than authored, and this comment used to say that is why every county has one: 433 of 434 do. County 4 of slot 8 (Africa) has no tile with flags == 0 at all - its 57 tiles are all road, boundary, rough, plot, farmland, town or site - so the original local_14 stays 0, its guard refuses, and that county can never make a weapon. Measured over all 44 shipped maps in crates/l2-scenario/tests/newgame.rs. See decisions.md C62. |
| `0x004701AC` | `Move_ExtractPath(who, x, y)` | verified | The campaign path extractor (distinct from the battle's Path_Extract at 0x00472392). Greedy descent down a campaign distance field from (x, y) back to the source, appending (x, y) pairs into g_pathBuf[who] with the count in g_pathLen[who]. On a cost-1 road tile it steps the direction index by 2, orthogonals only, and falls back to all eight when that finds nothing. |
| `0x00468D4F` | `Counties_PlaceSites()` | verified | Once at map load, for every county 1..g_countyCount: County_FindTownTile into +0x70 and the anchor +0x6C/+0x6D, then County_PlaceResourceSites and County_PlaceBlacksmith, then County_FindCastleTile into +0x78 and the castle tile +0x74/+0x75. The order matters - the resource sites are placed relative to the town, and the castle plot is found afterwards. |
| `0x00467FD1` | `County_FindTownTile(county)` | verified | Scans the 64x64 grid for this county's tiles with plane-0 bit **0x40** and no terrain yet, takes the 2x2 group, and leaves its x/y in DAT_0057C8D0 / DAT_0056846C. The result becomes the county anchor that County_FindFreeRoadTile searches around. Bit 0x40 is the county town: TileInfo_Draw prints L2.eng group 30 string 7 'County town.' for it, and the mercenary offer at county +0x1AD is advertised on it. See decisions.md C25. |
| `0x00468121` | `County_FindCastleTile(county)` | verified | As County_FindTownTile but for plane-0 bit **0x80**, and it also stamps terrain 0x14 on the 2x2 it finds, saving the previous terrain frame to tile +6. Its result is county +0x74/+0x75, which is exactly the tile FUN_004A79A3 moves a garrisoning army onto - so bit 0x80 is the castle. Unit_TryEnterTile's special case for a 0x80 tile whose terrain is 0x14 is this stamp. See decisions.md C25. |
| `0x0046D42C` | `County_FindDefendingArmy(county)` | verified | The existing-defender search Army_AttackCounty calls before levying one. Scans the 4x4 tile block x in [townX-2, townX+1], y in [townY-2, townY+1] around the county's town anchor (+0x6C/+0x6D), reads the unit index out of the occupancy plane at g_tiles+5, and returns the LARGEST type-1 unit whose owner equals the county's owner - 0 if there is none. The window arithmetic closes exactly: +8 a column, +0x1E0 to the next row = 512 - 4*8. Because the town is a 2x2 whose bottom-right corner is the anchor, the block is the town plus the one-tile ring around it: an army defends its county town by standing on or beside it. Corrects docs/armies.md section 9, which modelled it as 'the lowest-numbered army of the county's owner standing in the county'; both the scope and the tie-break were wrong, and the two readings disagree on battle-before.sav, where county 2's garrison sits at (30,46) and county 2's town is at (31,50). |
| `0x004ABA5A` | `Defence_Disband(unit)` | verified | Undoes a county defence after the battle, keyed on unit +0x167. Mark 1 (raised on the spot): the survivors go back into the home county's population and popArmy and the unit is destroyed. Mark 2 (an existing army pressed into the role): the mark is cleared and the army stays. Verified against the battle fixture triple - battle-during.sav slot 6 is the raised defence (owner 6, mark 1, 182 men) and county 3's population falls 728 to 582 across before/after, which is the 182 levied minus 36 survivors returned. That makes docs/armies.md section 8.1's [D] reading of +0x167 a data-side [V]. |
| `0x004AD39A` | `Armies_ReturnDefences()` | verified | Sweeps unit slots 1..150: runs Defence_Disband on every type-1 unit, then destroys any whose county byte is above 16 - an army standing outside the map's county set. Note the comma operator in the condition: the disband runs for every army, the destroy only for the strays. |
| `0x004686A0` | `Unit_ReachCastleBuilding(unit, tileOffset)` | verified | Unit_Step's code-6 branch for terrain 0x15..0x19, a built castle on a plane-0 bit 0x80 tile. Armies only: the county is yours -> Army_Garrison, otherwise -> Army_BeginSiege. Two unrelated functions agree on the test, which is what makes this [V] rather than [D] - Map_HoverUnitTarget collects the same tile as a garrison target when the county owner matches the unit's and as a siege target when it does not, using bit 0x80 and terrain above 0x14. The stepper additionally caps at below 0x1A; the hover has no upper bound. |
| `0x004A79A3` | `Army_GarrisonApply(unit, county)` | verified | The body Army_Garrison, its confirm-dialog callback and two other call sites all funnel into; Army_Garrison itself only stashes the arguments. Over g_castleGarrisonCap[castleType] it writes unit +0x1A = 2 and refuses; otherwise it links county +0x1BC and unit +0x198, teleports the unit onto the castle tile (county +0x74/+0x75), charges 5 moves and writes unit +0x1A = 5. An already-garrisoned castle takes Army_Combine instead. battle-before.sav slot 4 is a garrison and carries +0x1A = 5 at exactly county 2's +0x74/+0x75, which is the data-side confirmation docs/armies.md section 1.5 wanted for +0x1A. |
| `0x004A8AAB` | `Siege_LaunchAssault(unit)` | verified | Turn phase 2's payoff. Sets g_battleCounty from the besieger's +0x199, works out the castle level to fight (castleDegraded 1 with a build in progress -> castleBuilding-1; castleDegraded 2 -> county +0x1F9; else castleType-1), sums the three siege-engine counts at unit +0x182 + e*6, and fights only when the level is below 3 OR at least one engine was built. Otherwise message 0x119 - L2.eng group 281, 'Cannot siege castle' / 'Your captains advise that you must build some siege engines to besiege this castle.' - and Siege_Break lifts the siege. The engine offset closes: 0x52F232 minus g_units 0x52F0B0 is 0x182, stride 6, three records. |
| `0x004A84BA` | `Siege_TickPhase()` | verified | Turn phase 2's pump. Walks the resumable cursor g_siegeCursor from 1 to 150 calling Siege_BuildTick, and returns 1 the moment one reports its engines finished - leaving the cursor where it is, so the turn machine can run Siege_LaunchAssault on that unit and resume afterwards. Returns 0 when the sweep is exhausted. Siege_StartPhase seeds the cursor with 1. |
| `0x004A8426` | `Siege_ValidateLink(unit)` | verified | Clears unit +0x199 when the besieged county no longer has a garrison, or when that garrison is no longer garrisoned in that county, and counts the survivors into g_siegeCount. Run over every besieging army by Siege_StartPhase, and once more by Map_Click before it decides whether clicking your own army opens its move order or the siege-preparation screen. This corrects the existing comment on Siege_StartPhase, which says it 're-prepares every besieging army': it never calls Siege_Prepare, it breaks stale links. |
| `0x0043B917` | `Siege_Break(unit)` | verified | Lifts a siege: clears the besieger's +0x199 and the garrison's +0x19A. Its three callers are together the whole answer to docs/armies.md section 9's 'the lift-siege handler was not traced'. Unit_OrderMove calls it on every successful type-1 move order, so giving a besieging army anywhere to go lifts its siege; Siege_LaunchAssault calls it when the castle is too strong to assault without engines; and the siege-preparation screen's 'Lift siege' button (L2.eng 83/6) calls it directly. L2.eng 10/13 'Lift the siege?' is the confirmation the move-order path asks first. |
| `0x004A9252` | `Map_ConfirmMoveOrder()` | verified | Clicking a destination for the selected unit. Saves g_screenId into g_screenIdSaved, then picks at most one confirmation from the targets Map_HoverUnitTarget collected, in this priority: g_hoverVillageCounty -> dialog 4 'Slaughter villagers?'; g_hoverFieldCounty -> 10 'Destroy field?'; g_hoverMergeUnit -> 5 'Combine armies?'; g_hoverGarrisonCounty -> 7 'Garrison castle?'; g_hoverSiegeCounty -> 8 'Besiege castle?'; otherwise no dialog and the order goes straight through. | `0x004A960F` | `MoveOrder_ConfirmSiege()` | verified | The 'Besiege castle?' callback. No garrison and castleDegraded 0 -> message 0x11D = L2.eng 285, 'This castle is deserted my liege. | `0x004A975D` | `MoveOrder_ConfirmCombine()` | verified | The 'Combine armies?' callback, and the third independent sighting of the 1500 cap: it tests the two armies' men against 0x5DD before the order is issued at all, and otherwise sends message 0x112 = L2.eng 274, 'The resulting army would be over their recommended limit of 1500 troops.' | `0x0046F68E` | `Move_ResetSelection(realm, unit, x, y)` | verified | Clears the realm's distance field and re-seeds the movement preview at (x, y). Also writes the origin pair at 0x00504020/0x00504024 and the flat index y*64+x at 0x004F4070, then clears the local player's path buffer. The unit argument is not used. |
| `0x004A8D9C` | `Map_SelectUnitForMove()` | verified | Points g_moveTargetX/Y at the selected unit's own tile and calls Move_ResetSelection, so a freshly selected unit starts with a zero-length path rather than inheriting the previous unit's. |
| `0x0043723A` | `Map_BeginMoveSelection()` | verified | Enters the campaign map in move-order mode for g_selectedUnit: rebuilds the cost map, screen 0x10, resets the preview, sets 0x00553ECC to 40 (a counter Map_HoverUnitTarget decrements each frame), CLEARS unit +0x14E (ignoreSettlements) and runs Move_FloodFill from the unit. | `0x004371CE` | `Panel_MoveButton()` | verified | The unit panel's move button. | `0x0043733A` | `Panel_DisbandButton()` | verified | Picks the county the army would disband into - its home county, or the county it is standing in if the home county is no longer the owner's - and asks L2.eng 10/6 'Disband army?' only when that county is the owner's. Otherwise message 0x91 = L2.eng group 145: 'Your army must disband to its county of origin. If you no longer rule the county, the army must then disband inside a county that you do rule.' | `0x0043744F` | `Army_DisbandConfirm()` | verified | The 'Disband army?' yes-callback: Army_Disband, or network command 0x2E under g_multiplayer. |
| `0x00438681` | `Army_Disband(unit)` | verified | Releases any mercenary band, returns troops[1..6] to the realm's weapons[0..5] - troop type t maps to weapon slot t-1, the same off-by-one Levy_Init uses in the other direction - and returns the men to the county's population, its labour[8].workers and its popArmy. Then Army_Destroy, clear the garrison link, recompute the realm wage bill and re-run the labour allocator and Ration_Apply three times over. | `0x00437FD7` | `Army_Split(realm, army, destCounty, destTileOffset)` | verified | The army-division screen's yes-arm. Finds a free tile within five of the parent (FUN_0046733C), spawns a daughter army there and gives it the parent's morale, shield and county but NOT its homeCounty - Unit_Spawn has just zeroed that, so a split army's county of origin is 0 and Army_Disband falls through to wherever it is standing. Each side's troops come from one half of the levy basket: parent from slot[t].chosen, daughter from slot[t].available, and menTotal is the sum of all EIGHT slots so the mercenary band is counted. Both halves get moving = 2. With no destination county both pay movesUsed += 5 - the Readme's 'splitting does not use all the movement for a turn' - and with one, the daughter is given the destination tile and a flood-filled path instead. A band the daughter's column holds moves whole, g_mercBands[band].hiredBy included. |
| `0x004378B3` | `Panel_SplitButton()` | verified | The unit panel's split button, and the gate the Readme states: 'An army normally can only be split only at the start of its movement in a turn.' Otherwise it seeds the levy basket from the army - slot[t].chosen = troops[t], slot[t].available = 0, slot 7 = mercMen with its available at 0 - sums the two totals into DAT_00554468 and DAT_00554040, and sets g_screenId = 0x11. FUN_004376BB is the same seeding reached from the move-order confirm, for a split INTO a castle. |
| `0x00437AFB` | `Army_SplitConfirm()` | verified | The army-division screen's OK. | `0x00437D65` | `SplitScreen_ToParent()` | verified | Moves one man from the daughter's column back to the parent's on the army-division screen: slot[hotspot].available -= 1, slot[hotspot].chosen += 1, then both totals are resummed over all eight slots. | `0x00437E9E` | `SplitScreen_ToDaughter()` | verified | The twin of SplitScreen_ToParent: one man from the parent's column into the daughter's, and hotspot 7 moves the whole mercenary band. |
| `0x00435B4D` | `Army_RaiseConfirm()` | verified | The raise-army screen's yes-callback, and the only door to Army_Create a player has. | `0x00435CEF` | `Levy_SliderClick()` | verified | The levy slider on screen 0x17. Its hit band is x in [0x80, 0x176) and y in [base + 0x10, base + 0x40), where base is 0x80 with a mercenary offer in the county and 0xA0 without - the same local Screen_RaiseArmy lays the whole screen out from. | `0x004A7948` | `Army_GarrisonConfirm()` | verified | Yes-callback of the confirmation Army_Garrison stashes its arguments for: restores g_screenId from g_screenIdSaved and runs Army_GarrisonApply, or sends network command 0x34 under g_multiplayer. | `0x004A7DAF` | `Army_BeginSiegeConfirm()` | verified | Yes-callback for 'Besiege castle?': Siege_Link, or network command 0x35 under g_multiplayer. |
| `0x0043EDA0` | `Net_SendCommand(op, dest)` | verified | The multiplayer command sender, and the reason every campaign action in the binary is written twice - once as a direct call and once as Net_SendCommand(op, 0) under g_multiplayer. Looks the opcode up in g_netCmdLength (0xFF is not a command and errors), calls g_netCmdWriters[op] to serialise the payload, stamps opcode, length, checksum and a sequence number that wraps 1..39 into the matching 0x104-byte slot of g_netSendRing, and sends the packet once for eleven listed opcodes and three times - with flag bits 0x40 then 0x80 - for all the others. Two invariants close: all 112 entries of g_netCmdWriters are real function starts, and opcode 0x29's writer emits 1+4+1+2+2 bytes, exactly the 10 g_netCmdLength records for it. The campaign-map opcodes are 0x29 move order, 0x2C move-and-combine, 0x2E disband, 0x34 garrison, 0x35 begin siege, 0x3A toggle industry from the map. |
| `0x004401BA` | `Net_WriteField(src, len)` | verified | Appends len bytes to the command packet being built. Its reader twin is Net_ReadField, and the two come as matched pairs: the writer for opcode N is g_netCmdWriters[N] and its reader is the next function in address order, ending where g_netCmdWriters[N+1] begins. That pairing plus the length table is what makes a command's payload readable without running anything. |
| `0x004401F6` | `Net_ReadField(dest, len)` | verified | Reads len bytes back out of a received command packet. See Net_WriteField. |
| `0x00443990` | `NetCmd_WriteMoveOrder()` | verified | g_netCmdWriters[0x29]: g_localPlayer (1), a 4-byte value from FUN_00446E21, g_selectedUnit (1), g_moveTargetX (2), g_moveTargetY (2) - ten bytes, matching g_netCmdLength[0x29] exactly. Its reader at 0x00443A2D deserialises the same five fields in the same order and both then call the command journal at 0x00447136 with sub-opcode 0x0C. This is the whole payload of a campaign move order, and therefore the whole input a lockstep peer needs to reproduce one. |
| `0x00465214` | `Unit_TickNone()` | verified | g_unitTickTable[0]: an eleven-byte function that returns. Dead - Units_Tick sends kind 0 to the record-clearer before it indexes the table, so slot 0 is never called. Named so the table reads as six entries of which two (0 and 5) are inert, rather than as a gap. |
| `0x00465486` | `PeasantMob_Tick()` | verified | g_unitTickTable[2], the revolting-peasant tick, and the answer to which of the four unit types a given sweep serves. moveAllowance 10 (not the army's 15), sprite base -0x70 = 0x90 with the army's three-frame walk table, county recount on a border crossing, then FUN_004ABD0F and Unit_Step. It opens with `if (menTotal < 0x1E) destroy` - and that is the ONLY `menTotal < 0x1E` in the whole corpus, so the 'below 30 men it is destroyed on the map' rule in docs/armies.md section 0 belongs to peasant mobs, not to armies. Army_Tick has no such test, and L2.eng 148, which that row cites, only says 'impractical to create an army of less than 50 men'. |
| `0x00465622` | `Merchant_Tick()` | verified | g_unitTickTable[3]. moveAllowance 10, county byte assigned from the tile with no border event at all - a merchant crossing a frontier greets nobody and triggers no recount - then the cooldown decrement and Unit_Step. Its sprite is facing*6 plus g_merchantWalkFrames[walkPhase], a SIX-frame cycle over eight facings rather than the army's three, and the arithmetic closes at 47. Verified on the battle fixtures three for three: merchants at facing 6, 6 and 2 carry spriteFrame 42, 42 and 18, and ((facing+1)&7)*6 gives exactly those. |
| `0x00465761` | `Transport_Tick()` | verified | g_unitTickTable[4], and it is Merchant_Tick again - same 319 bytes, same body, statement for statement. A transport ticks exactly like a merchant; the two types differ only in what the arrival handlers do with them. Worth naming separately because the duplication is the finding: a reimplementation that shares one tick between types 3 and 4 is faithful, and one that invents a difference is not. |
| `0x004AAD07` | `Battle_AutoResolve()` | verified | The autocalc - the whole of a battle nobody watches, and one of only two ways a battle can end. Clears g_battleWithdrawal, takes both sides' Army_StrengthScore, multiplies the DEFENDER's by 160/200/250/320/400 per cent by castle level on a siege, forms ratio = PctOf(larger, smaller) and reads a survival percentage out of the ten-pair ladder g_autocalcSurvivalLadder (default 100). The winner is A unless strengthA < strengthB, so a TIE GOES TO THE ATTACKER. The winner keeps Pct(count, p) of each of its seven troop counts and of its mercenary band and has +0x168 rebuilt by summing rather than scaling; the loser's seven counts are zeroed, its band released and its total set to 0. Reproduces battle-after.sav exactly: 926 against 1044 is a ratio of 112, the ladder's second rung is 20 per cent, and 20 per cent of 122 peasants and 60 archers is the 36 men county 3's population gains back. |
| `0x004A6A30` | `Battle_ChooseSettlement()` | verified | Decides which of three ways a battle is settled, and is called by all three battle entries (Army_AttackCounty, the army-versus-army path FUN_004A7158, and Siege_LaunchAssault). Returns 0 when NEITHER side's ownerIsHuman is set - an AI-versus-AI battle is autocalculated with no screen at all. Otherwise it returns 1, sets g_battleBothHuman when both sides are human, records which player owns the take-the-field choice in g_battleChoiceOwner, and copies both owners and shields for the screens; each caller then tests (g_optFightHumansOnly == 0 && !bothHuman) to autocalc straight to the report screen 0x13, or pushes the prompt screen 0x12 and asks. There is no distance test, no army-size cutoff and no separate quick-battle toggle on this path. |
| `0x00429418` | `Transport_RetargetAll()` | verified | Turn phase 3, step 1. For every type-4 unit, unconditionally: destX/destY = the anchor of the unit's cargo county (+0x167), destCounty = that county, hasNoDestination = 0, then the two-pass path (Move_FloodFill mode 1, retried at mode 0 if the road route needs 150 or more waypoints) and moving = 1. It does not test hasNoDestination, which is what lets a transport resume a journey it could not finish last season. The destination is the anchor itself, not a free tile near it - that is the merchant's rule and the two differ. |
| `0x004AC499` | `PeasantMob_AdvanceAll()` | verified | Turn phase 5, step 1. For every type-2 unit, calls PeasantMob_PickDestination and, if it succeeds, flood-fills (mode 0, no road preference), extracts the path and sets moving = 1. |
| `0x004AC5BA` | `PeasantMob_PickDestination(unit)` | verified | Picks a mob's destination from g_mobCountyCursor, a single counter shared by every mob: bump it, wrap past g_countyCount back to 1, and bump once more if it names the county the mob is standing in - so consecutive mobs go to consecutive counties and a mob is never sent where it already is. Re-targets only when g_season == 2 or destCounty == 0. Note the destination coordinates are read from the cursor's county while destCounty is the unit's own field, so outside season 2 a mob that kept its old destCounty walks to a different county's anchor than the one it believes it is going to. |
| `0x004A4F5B` | `Units_AnyMovingOfKind(kind)` | verified | The wait predicate for turn phases 3 and 6. Walks slots 1..150 and, for every unit of this kind with moving != 0, sets moving = 2 and returns 1. It is not a read-only query: it promotes 'ordered' (1) to 'stepping' (2) as well as reporting that the phase is still busy, so one call both starts a phase's units and waits on them. |
| `0x004A4E3D` | `Units_AnyMovingOfKindForRealm(kind, realm)` | verified | As Units_AnyMovingOfKind but also requires owner == realm and ownerIsHuman == 0, and calls Siege_Break on any type-1 unit it starts. Two callers: turn phase 5's wait, as (2, 6) - so the phase waits only on realm 6's ownerless mobs - and the AI turn's finish test, as (1, realm), which is why a realm with armies still walking keeps stepping past its aiStep threshold. Promoted from the hypothesis Unit_AbandonOrders, whose name had it backwards: it starts orders, it does not abandon them. |
| `0x004A7158` | `Battle_BeginFromCampaign(mover, occupant)` | verified | The army-versus-army battle entry, and the third caller of Battle_ChooseSettlement. Reached from Unit_EnterOccupiedTile when an army steps onto a tile held by a realm Diplo_ActionAllowed permits attacking. Sets g_battleIsSiege = 0, g_battleCounty from the OCCUPANT's county, g_battleArmyA = mover, g_battleArmyB = occupant, and clears both sides' battle scratch fields. Returns 1 only when an interactive battle screen was opened (g_screenId = 0x12); the caller then sets g_battleStartedLatch and puts back the waypoint the step consumed. Otherwise it auto-resolves through Battle_AutoResolve and Battle_ReturnToCampaign and returns 0. This is the campaign side of the seam: Army_AttackCounty and Siege_LaunchAssault are the other two ways into the same machinery. |
| `0x004A72FE` | `County_ChangeOwner(newOwner, county)` | verified | Hands a county to a new realm: recomputes the taker's totals, raises one of the nine "we have taken" / "we have lost" letters (L2.eng 0x72..0x7E, chosen on the taker's county count and share of the map), calls Realm_RecountStrength on the OLD owner, writes the new owner, and docks the county happiness by the conquest penalty. [V] The recount happens one statement before the owner is written, so the county being lost is still counted and the losing realm cannot be eliminated here - it survives until its own AI_RunTurnStep step 0. |
| `0x0043B681` | `SiegePrep_OrderMore()` | verified | Siege-preparation screen, the increment half of an engine row. Picks its ceiling from g_uiHotspotId - 4 for hotspots 0 and 1 (catapults, siege towers) and 2 for anything else (battering rams) - and refuses to increment at it. Under g_multiplayer it sends network command 0x37 instead. The ceiling is the screen's and not the record's: Siege_Prepare writes 4 towers for the Knight and 3 catapults plus 2 towers for the Countess without consulting it. Ceiling times g_siegeEngineWork is 800 man-seasons for all three rows, which is a second statement that the table is 200/200/400 in record order. |
| `0x0043B741` | `SiegePrep_OrderFewer()` | verified | Siege-preparation screen, the decrement half of an engine row. Refuses at zero; network command 0x37 under g_multiplayer. |
| `0x0043B7C4` | `SiegePrep_AdjustOrder(unit, engine, delta)` | verified | Adds delta to the engine record at unit +0x182 + engine*6 and runs Siege_RecomputeBuildTime, so the seasons-remaining line moves as the player clicks. The tail of both SiegePrep_OrderMore and SiegePrep_OrderFewer. |
| `0x0043B848` | `SiegePrep_Button()` | verified | The siege-preparation screen's two buttons. Hotspot 1 is L2.eng 83/6 'Lift siege': it closes the screen and calls Siege_Break (network command 0x38). Hotspot 2 is 83/7 'Proceed': it closes the screen and launches the assault ONLY IF unit +0x19C is already zero (network command 0x39), so proceeding with work outstanding simply leaves turn phase 2 to carry the build on. |
| `0x0043B98A` | `SiegePrep_Proceed(unit)` | verified | A one-line thunk to Siege_LaunchAssault, called by SiegePrep_Button when the engines are ready. |
| `0x00497E65` | `PlayerStart_Shuffle()` | verified | Re-deals the live entries of g_playerStartTable into each other slots, from Game_NewGame between Mercenary_Init and PlayerStart_Compact. For each source entry i it draws a random offset, pos = (rand & 3) + 1 + i wrapping at the live count, then probes forward for a free destination. [V] THIS IS WHY WHICH REALM YOU PLAY IS DIFFERENT EVERY GAME: docs/environment.md records the realm-to-county assignment as rolled per game, from two independently created England turn-one saves disagreeing about it, and this is the code that rolls it. The start counties themselves never move - they are the map plane-4 markers. Its second half deals a 48-entry table into DAT_0057CAE0 and fills DAT_00553080 with 0, 100 or 6 + 5n per entry; neither destination has been traced and neither is a player start. Reproduced in l2_scenario::newgame::shuffle_starts on our own Pcg32 - the shape is the original, the stream cannot be. See decisions.md C62. |
| `0x0046826C` | `Castle_StampTile(county, level)` | verified | Draws the castle onto the map, and the frame comes from the castle's STATE: castleDegraded == 0 -> level*4 + 0x50 (finished), else +0x1C4 < 0x32 -> level*4 + 0x28 (scaffolding), else level*4 + 0x3C (half built). content = 0x15 + level. Map_StampBlock(frame, 2, county.castleTile, 0x10, content), so bank bits 0x10 = bank index 4 = Castle1a.pl8 / Castle2a.pl8 - a seasonal bank, a..d, swapped whole by Gfx_LoadCountyMode. Called by Castle_Order and by Castle_BuildTick every season, which is why a castle changes picture as it goes up. This is the only writer of a castle's artwork: L2_maps.dat holds plain ground on the plot. Promotes hypotheses.json H6 - the content ladder is read from the writer, not from two saves. |
| `0x00468349` | `Castle_ClearTile` | verified | The inverse of Castle_StampTile over the same 2x2: restore each tile's frame from its saved frame at +6, clear bank bits 0x1C, re-set bit 8 when plane-0 bit 2 is set, and write content 0x14 - the bare plot. |
| `0x0046841C` | `Castle_EvictTile` | verified | Called by Castle_Order right after Castle_StampTile: walks the castle's 2x2 and, for any unit standing on it, calls Unit_UnlinkFromTile and sets unit +0x14E. Ordering a castle turns its plot into an obstacle at once, so whatever was standing there is pushed out of the tile chain. |
| `0x0046AC22` | `Map_StampBlock(baseFrame, size, tileOffset, bankBits, content)` | verified | Writes an n x n block of tile records. Per tile: bank \|= 1, bank &= 0xE3, frame = baseFrame + g_blockQuadrants[i] for the given size (size 1 writes baseFrame flat), bank \|= bankBits, content = content. Steps 8 bytes a tile across and (0x40 - size)*8 at the end of a row. The town, the resource sites and the castle all go through it. |
| `0x004A551B` | `Castle_RaiseFreeGarrison(county)` | verified | The garrison a finished castle comes with. archers = g_castleFreeArchers[castleType-1], minus g_castleFreeArchers[castleBuilding-1] when castleBuilding != 0 - so an upgrade yields only the DIFFERENCE and a motte-and-bailey to a Norman keep yields nothing. Refuses when that is <= 0 or above population/2. Otherwise clears the realm's levy basket, adds the archers to realm.weapons[4] so they cost nothing, writes them into basket slot 5 (archers) and slot 7 (the total), and calls Army_Create. Its one caller is Castle_BuildTick, which passes the result to Army_GarrisonApply. |
| `0x0046EDDF` | `Unit_LinkToTile` | verified | Puts a unit into its tile's occupancy chain (tile +5, chained through unit +0x04). It opens 'if (kind != 1 \|\| garrisonCounty == 0)' and does nothing otherwise, so A GARRISONED ARMY IS NEVER ON ITS TILE. With Army_GarrisonApply's Unit_UnlinkFromTile call that is two functions agreeing, and it is what stops a besieger meeting the garrison as an obstacle and fighting a field battle instead of laying a siege - Unit_TryEnterTile tests occupancy before any tile flag. |
| `0x0046EC6E` | `Unit_UnlinkFromTile` | verified | Removes a unit from its tile's occupancy chain, walking the +0x04 links with a ten-step guard. Called by Army_GarrisonApply on the way into a castle and by Castle_EvictTile. |
| `0x004A5667` | `Ai_AdvanceArmies(realm)` | verified | AI turn step 11. Walks slots 1..150 and, for every type-1 unit of this realm whose mission handler (Ai_RunMission, 0x004A57AC) returns non-zero, clears +0x14E, floods from the unit in mode 0 and re-paths it to destX/destY. Byte for byte the same shape as the verified PeasantMob_AdvanceAll. Note mode 0: the AI driver does NOT prefer roads, though Unit_OrderMove does. |
| `0x004A57AC` | `Ai_RunMission(unit)` | verified | Dispatches on unit +0x1A, the AI mission byte: 7 to 0x004A58F4, 6 to 0x004A599D, 2 to 0x004A5B1F, 3 to 0x004A5F0A, 4 to 0x004A6270, 5 to 0x004A60B9, and anything else rewrites +0x1A to 2 and returns 0. Non-zero means the handler wrote destX/destY and wants a fresh path. docs/armies.md section 10 has the enum; this closes +0x1A, which docs/armies.md section 1.5 had as a UI feedback code. |
| `0x004A58F4` | `Ai_MissionRaid(unit)` | verified | Mission 7. Unless the unit is besieging (+0x199), re-aims it at the nearest STANDING CROP of its destCounty via Ai_FindStandingCropTile and clears needsDestination. It is re-aimed every turn, which is what makes a fifty-man unarmed raiding party destructive: Unit_TrampleTile fires on every field it crosses in a county it does not own. |
| `0x004A599D` | `Ai_MissionAssistAlly(unit)` | verified | Mission 6. Re-validates unit +0x19B, the ally-help target county, through 0x0049FF71; a cancelled request demotes the unit to mission 2 and runs that handler in the SAME tick. Otherwise it closes on the nearest enemy army in that county, but only within Chebyshev 21 - outside that it does nothing at all this turn. |
| `0x004A5B1F` | `Ai_MissionSeekEnemy(unit)` | verified | Mission 2, the default war mission and the one every unrecognised value becomes. Four arms in order: intercept an enemy army within Chebyshev 6 (tie-break: the more westerly gives chase, and an army already on mission 2 is not chased); keep the standing destCounty if it is still hostile and still borders the realm; else pick a new target with 0x004A0649; else attack the county it is standing in. Two defects: the last arm never updates destCounty, and an ally-owned county with no alternative aims the army at county 0. It also calls 0x00467F2E and discards the result - the two arms it was written to choose between are identical. |
| `0x004A5F0A` | `Ai_MissionHoldHome(unit)` | verified | Mission 3. Intercepts the nearest attackable army in the unit homeCounty (+0x11) within Chebyshev 31 - five times mission 2 radius - and otherwise walks back to that county. Always returns 1 unless besieging. Its only setter is 0x0049F431, which overwrites homeCounty with the realm muster county on the abandon branch, so home means posted-to rather than raised-in. |
| `0x004A6270` | `Ai_MissionJoinGarrison(unit)` | verified | Mission 4. Re-checks that the target castle has room for the WHOLE army (g_castleGarrisonCap tested against the unit men plus the sitting garrison), is standing, is not under construction and still belongs to the realm; failing any of those it looks for another castle with room (0x004A07F2) and disbands the army if there is none. Returns 1 even after Army_Disband, so the caller then floods from a record Army_Destroy has already cleared. |
| `0x004A60B9` | `Ai_MissionGarrisoned(unit)` | verified | Mission 5, which Army_GarrisonApply writes. Does nothing while destCounty still belongs to the unit owner. Once the county is lost the garrison is ejected (0x00437535) and demoted to mission 2, and in the one case where the castle is still standing and unruined it is also sent at the county town to take it back. |
| `0x004A64CA` | `Ai_AimAtCounty(unit, county)` | verified | The choice missions 2 and 3 make between the two county tiles: a county with no castle, or a castle with no garrison, is entered at the TOWN (Ai_FindTownTile); anything else is approached at the CASTLE (Ai_FindCastleBuildingTile), which begins a siege. L2.eng group 286 states the same rule to the player. |
| `0x004A545F` | `Levy_EquipSlot(realm, slot)` | verified | Moves min(unequipped men, weapons remaining in that basket slot) from basket slot 0 into slot 1..6 - it arms as many still-unarmed levies with that one weapon type as the stock allows. Called five times by 0x004A5389 in the order 5, 1, 3, 2, 6: bows, crossbows, swords, maces, mail. Pikes, slot 4, are never issued by that path. |

| `0x0052AFAC` | `g_siegeCount` | verified | How many sieges survived Siege_StartPhase's link validation this turn. Zeroed there and incremented once per surviving besieger by Siege_ValidateLink. |
