| `0x00554000` | `g_siegeScreenCounty` | verified | The county Army_BeginSiege stashed for its confirmation callback, which passes it back to Siege_Link. |
| `0x004D5B90` | `g_netCmdLength` | verified | u8[112]: the payload length of each network command, 0xFF for an opcode that is not one. Valid opcodes run 0x01..0x61; 0x00 and 0x62..0x6F are all 0xFF. Net_SendCommand rejects a 0xFF opcode outright. Opcode 0x29's 10 matches its writer's 1+4+1+2+2 exactly, which is what makes the table's reading a finding rather than a guess. |
| `0x004D57F0` | `g_netCmdWriters` | verified | void(*)(void)[112], the per-opcode payload serialisers Net_SendCommand calls. Every one of the 112 entries is a real function start in the decompiled corpus - a mechanical check that could have failed - and each writer's matching reader is the next function in address order, ending where the next opcode's writer begins. The campaign-map entries are 0x29 NetCmd_WriteMoveOrder, 0x2C move-and-combine, 0x2E disband, 0x34 garrison, 0x35 begin siege, 0x3A toggle industry. |
| `0x00512FB0` | `g_netSendRing` | verified | The outgoing packet ring Net_SendCommand builds into: FORTY slots of 0x104 bytes, 0x00512FB0..0x0051584F, of which the sequence counter g_netSendSlot uses only 1..39 - slot 0 is allocated and never written. The extent is arithmetic that closes exactly: 0x00512FB0 + 40 * 0x104 = 0x00515850, and 0x00515850 is the next address anything in the binary references - nothing at all is referenced between +0x104 and there. Slot layout: +0 opcode, +1 the declared payload length from g_netCmdLength, +2 a checksum, +3 the sequence number, which the triple-send path ORs with 0x40 and then 0x80 to mark the two duplicates; +4.. the payload, written through the cursor g_netSendCursor. The checksum at 0x00440038 sums length bytes starting at slot+4, and the sender is handed length+4 bytes from the slot base, so the four header bytes and the payload account for the whole packet. Settles a name recorded twice: the battle pass called this g_netSendRing and the campaign-map pass g_netPacketBuf, and they disagreed 39 against 40 because those are two different true counts - forty slots exist, thirty-nine are in rotation. |
| `0x00512F4C` | `g_netSendSlot` | verified | The g_netSendRing slot the next packet goes in, and the sequence number stamped into its +3. Net_SendCommand increments it after each command and resets it to 1 when it passes 0x27, so it cycles 1..39 and slot 0 of the forty-slot ring is never used. FUN_0043F01D, the session reset, writes it twice while opening a game. This wrap test is what fixes the ring's slot count. |
| `0x00512F8C` | `g_netSendCursor` | verified | Where the next byte of the packet payload goes. Net_SendCommand points it at g_netSendRing slot + 4 before calling the opcode's writer, and Net_WriteField appends through it and advances it - two sites, a writer and its reader, and nothing else touches it. The +4 is the same four header bytes the checksum at 0x00440038 skips. |
| `0x0053FB72` | `g_county0_castleRuined` | verified | County +0x1C2. Non-zero blocks both garrisoning and besieging: Army_BeginSiege refuses silently, and MoveOrder_ConfirmGarrison refuses with message 0xA5 = L2.eng 165, 'You cannot station men in a ruined castle.' The string is what names the field. |
| `0x004D6AB8` | `g_merchantWalkFrames` | verified | i32[6] = {0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5}, the walk-frame cycle Merchant_Tick and Transport_Tick index with unit +0x1B. Six frames per facing against the army's three-frame ping-pong at g_unitWalkFrames, and the sprite arithmetic closes: ((facing+1)&7)*6 + phase covers 0..47 with nothing left over. The three merchants in the battle fixtures sit at frames 42, 42 and 18, which is that formula at phase 0 for facings 6, 6 and 2. |
| `0x004DE710` | `g_autocalcSurvivalLadder` | verified | Ten (threshold, value) int32 pairs, the ladder Battle_AutoResolve reads the winner's survival percentage from with Table_Lookup(ratio, .., 10, 100): 110/10, 130/20, 160/30, 180/40, 220/50, 270/65, 360/80, 500/90, 700/95, 900/98. Read straight out of Lords2.exe; the thresholds ascend strictly and the eleventh pair in memory is (100, 200), a different table starting, which is what fixes the count at ten. An evenly matched fight - ratio under 110 - leaves the winner ONE TENTH of its army, so mutual annihilation is the default outcome of auto-resolving a fair battle, and only a roughly threefold advantage is cheap. |
| `0x0056822C` | `g_battleCounty` | verified | The county a battle is being fought over. Written by Army_AttackCounty (the county attacked), by the army-versus-army path (the defender's county byte) and by Siege_LaunchAssault (the besieged county); read by Battle_ReturnToCampaign to decide which county changes hands, and by both battle screens for its name. |
| `0x004E59DC` | `g_mobCountyCursor` | verified | The peasant mobs' shared destination cursor - one counter for the whole map, not one per mob. See PeasantMob_PickDestination. |
| `0x0050A49E` | `g_battleStartedLatch` | verified | Set by Unit_EnterOccupiedTile when Battle_BeginFromCampaign opens an interactive battle. Units_Tick returns as soon as it is set, so units in higher slots do not move on that tick at all, and Unit_StepOnce returns 10 so no tile is entered and no movement is charged. |
| `0x004D8AF8` | `g_aiSiegeDoctrine` | verified | Personality record +0xA0, stride 0xF0 from g_aiPersonality at 0x004D8A58, read by Siege_Prepare and by nothing else. The four shipped values are 8, 9, 7, 7 for the Knight, Baron, Countess and Bishop: 8 orders four siege towers, 9 one battering ram, 7 three catapults plus a ram against a stone or royal castle after season 2, and every arm keeps the default two towers. Three of the four values are exactly the three constants the function tests, and the fourth repeats one. docs/diplomacy.md section 8.4 listed +0xA0 among eleven personality fields that were never traced. |
| `0x004D80E0` | `g_blockQuadrants` | verified | Map_StampBlock's per-cell frame offsets, read out of the binary: size 2 at 0x004D80E0 is 0, 2, 1, 3 (north-west, north-east, south-west, south-east); size 3 at 0x004D80E8 is 0, 2, 5, 1, 4, 7, 3, 6, 8; size 4 at 0x004D80F8 begins 0, 2, 5, 9, 1, 4, 8, 12. |

<!-- END symbols.json: units -->

Unit types, from the `L2.eng` group 31 strings `UnitPanel_Draw` selects on them:
**1 = army, 2 = revolting peasants, 3 = merchant, 4 = transport**. Owner byte `6` means
nobody. The merchant half of this subsystem is written up in
[`formats/plane4.md`](formats/plane4.md).

## Battle simulation

The real-time battle. Two arrays at fixed addresses hold the whole thing: **80 units**
(`g_battleUnits`, stride `0x34`) - what the player selects and orders - and **80 figures**
(`g_battleMen`, stride `0x1B0`) - the drawn men, each standing for `g_menPerFigure` real
soldiers. Both are indexed 1 to 80; index 0 is the "none" value.

`BattleDebug_Panel` (`0x00424992`) is the developers own field-name overlay and is the
primary evidence for the field names below - it prints figure and unit fields beside
labels like `hits`, `state`, `dirc`, `re targ` and `orders`.

Written up in full in [`battle.md`](battle.md).

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: battle -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004778A0` | `Battle_Start()` | verified | Brings a battle up: builds the battlefield (Battlefield_BuildRandom, Battlefield_BuildFromSkr or Battlefield_BuildCastle), then Battle_InitArmies, then the first unit/figure update passes. See docs/battle.md. |
| `0x0047EFEE` | `Battle_InitArmies()` | verified | Chooses the battlefield size class from the two armies, clears the figure and unit arrays, then raises both sides. Also computes each side's autocalc strength as sum(count[t] * g_troopStrengthWeight[t]) over troop types 0..6. |
| `0x0047FEA7` | `Battle_RaiseSide(armyUnit, outnumbered, side)` | verified | Turns one army record into battle units. Walks the 11 troop types in g_raiseOrder, and for each splits the men into units of at most maxFigures*g_menPerFigure. Siege counts are multiplied by g_menPerFigure so that one engine is one figure. `side` is 4 or 0. |
| `0x004801F8` | `Battle_RaiseSideSiege(armyUnit, outnumbered, side)` | inferred | The castle-defender variant of Battle_RaiseSide: uses g_raiseOrderSiege (8 troop types, no catapult/tower/ram), forces archers and crossbowmen to at most 3 figures per unit, and places units by wall slot rather than by deployment marker. |
| `0x00480599` | `BattleUnit_LoadTroopStats(troopType)` | verified | Copies one row of g_troopBattleStats (0x004D96D0, stride 0x14) into scratch globals, then the matching row of g_missileStats (0x004D97B0) selected by the weapon class in field +0x0C. |
| `0x00480662` | `BattleUnit_Create(siegePlacement, troopType, outnumbered, side, men, owner, mercenary)` | verified | Allocates a battle unit and fills it with ceil(men / g_menPerFigure) figures laid out in a rectangle. Sets the unit category from the troop type, and copies the troop and missile stats into every figure. |
| `0x0046E4C8` | `BattleMan_Create(troopType, x0, dx, y0, dy, owner, unit)` | verified | Allocates the first free slot of the figure array g_battleMen (81 records, slots 1..80 usable) (stride 0x1B0), places it on the battlefield, writes its index into cell byte +5 and links it to its unit. Returns 0 when all 80 slots are in use, which silently truncates the army. |
| `0x0046E97A` | `BattleUnit_Alloc(owner, humanControlled)` | verified | Allocates the first free slot of the unit array g_battleUnits (81 records, slots 1..80 usable) (stride 0x34); a slot is free when byte +0 (owner) is zero. Leaves the index in g_lastBattleUnit. |
| `0x0046E767` | `Missile_Spawn(owner, x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | Allocates one of the 100 slots of g_missiles (stride 0x4C) and sets its start and target positions in 1/32-cell units. Used for arrows, bolts, catapult shot, thrown oil, fire and falling figures. |
| `0x0046EBAE` | `BattleUnit_Clear(rec)` | verified | Zeroes one 0x34-byte battle unit record. |
| `0x0046EB3F` | `BattleMan_Clear(rec)` | verified | Zeroes one 0x1B0-byte battle figure record. |
| `0x0046F0F0` | `BattleMen_ClearAll()` | verified | Zeroes g_battleMen[1..80]. The loop bound 0x51 is what fixes the figure count at 80. |
| `0x0046F18D` | `BattleUnits_ClearAll()` | verified | Zeroes g_battleUnits[1..80]. |
| `0x0046EBE4` | `BattleMan_Destroy(man)` | verified | Clears the figure off its battlefield cell and zeroes its record. |
| `0x004822ED` | `Battle_UpdateAllMen()` | verified | Per-frame sweep over g_battleMen[1..80]. Applies burning damage on surface 10/17, ages the figure, then dispatches g_troopTickTable[troopType] - which reloads the per-troop stats and tail-calls g_manStateTable[state]. |
| `0x00489401` | `Battle_UpdateAllUnits()` | verified | Per-frame sweep over g_battleUnits[1..80]. Recentres each unit on its figures, and runs its order handler unless byte +1 (human controlled) is set. Three dispatch tables: field, siege attacker, siege defender. Counts field +0x14 down from 500 and re-targets at zero. |
| `0x0047EBE8` | `Battle_UpdateMissiles()` | inferred | Rebuilds the cell -> figure occupancy map for all 80 figures, then steps every missile. |
| `0x00485BB1` | `Missile_UpdateAll()` | verified | Steps g_missiles[1..100]. |
| `0x00492C8B` | `Missile_Step()` | verified | Advances the current missile by its steps-per-tick and, when it enters a cell holding an enemy figure, applies the missile damage formula: elevation scaling, minus the target armour, minus the crossbow-vs-engine reduction, floor 2, accumulated into the target hits pool; every 100 hits kills one man. See docs/battle.md. |
| `0x00494908` | `Melee_Tick()` | verified | One tick of a melee duel. The attacker decrements the defender recovery counter; when it expires the defender takes the attacker melee attack value into its hits pool, and once per exchange the attacker heavy-blow bonus as well. 100 hits kills one man; the attacker role swaps when the exchange counter runs out. |
| `0x0049459A` | `BattleMan_BurnTick(man)` | inferred | Adds 3/6/9/12 hits per tick (1/3/5/7 for siege engines) by battlefield size class to a figure standing on a burning cell, +1/+2 more if the owner is human-controlled. Threshold 100 (160 for engines) kills one man. |
| `0x00481ABB` | `BattleMan_RecomputeStrength(man)` | verified | Sets the figure strength band 0..3 by comparing its remaining men against g_strengthBand1/2/3 (75%, 50%, ~19-25% of a full figure), then loads its melee attack value from g_meleeAttackTable[troopType][band]. |
| `0x0047F42D` | `Battle_LoadStrengthBands()` | verified | Loads the three strength-band thresholds for the current battlefield size class from g_strengthBandTable. |
| `0x00483337` | `BattleMan_FireMissile()` | verified | Missile reload and fire. Acquires a target 10 ticks before the reload interval expires, then spawns a missile whose power is the figure missile damage scaled by its strength band (full, 4/5, 3/4, 1/2). |
| `0x004832EA` | `BattleMan_StateIdle()` | verified | Figure state 5. Advances the animation, and fires if the figure has a missile weapon. |
| `0x00483CE1` | `BattleMan_LookForMelee()` | verified | Figure state 7. If an adjacent enemy exists, locks both figures into state 4 facing each other, one as attacker and one as defender; otherwise falls back to idle or missile search. |
| `0x004831D8` | `BattleMan_StateMelee()` | verified | Figure state 4. Drops out if the opponent is gone or dead, otherwise runs Melee_Tick. |
| `0x00494E3F` | `Melee_FindAdjacentEnemy()` | verified | Tests the eight neighbouring cells in the order N, NW, NE, W, E, SW, SE, S and returns the direction of the first live enemy figure, or 8. Siege engines (troop types 7..10) are never returned. |
| `0x004956CC` | `Missile_FindTarget(range, man, unit)` | verified | Nearest live enemy figure within `range` by Manhattan distance, capped at 160. The unit ordered target is forced to distance 0; siege-engine targets get a +35 distance penalty. |
| `0x00494D08` | `Melee_ResetExchange(man)` | inferred | Sets how long this figure holds the attacking role in a duel, from its animation class: 0, 40, 80, 80 or 120 ticks. |
| `0x00494DF9` | `Melee_ResetRecovery(man)` | inferred | Adds the figure recovery interval (+0x18B) to its recovery counter. That interval is the rate at which the figure can be hit in melee, so it is the real melee defence: peasants and archers 6 ticks, crossbowmen 8, macemen and swordsmen 12, knights 16, pikemen 30. |
| `0x0048F1DD` | `BattleMan_Step(noInterrupt)` | verified | Movement. Waits g_troopBattleStats[type].moveDelay ticks per sub-step, accumulates 2 per step until 17, then takes the next path direction and calls BattleMan_TryStepDir. Knights have delay 0 and pikemen 4, which is why knights are fastest and pikemen slowest. |
| `0x00490616` | `BattleMan_TryStepDir(dir)` | verified | Turns a direction 0..7 into a neighbouring cell offset, clamps at the map edge and calls Cell_TryEnter. |
| `0x00490A44` | `Cell_TryEnter(cellOffset)` | verified | Decides whether a figure may enter a battlefield cell: 1 free, 0 blocked by a friendly figure or flag 0x40, 2 impassable (flags 0x10/0x80, or an elevation change of more than 1), 5 flag 0x20 for a non-zero side, 999 enemy figure present. |
| `0x004972F9` | `Melee_AdjacentEnemyDir(x, y, cellOffset)` | verified | Scans the eight neighbours via g_cellNeighbourOffsets and returns the direction of an enemy figure standing at the same elevation, or 8. |
| `0x0046F434` | `Dir_FromDelta(x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | Eight-way direction from (x,y) to (tx,ty): 0 N, 1 NE, 2 E, 3 SE, 4 S, 5 SW, 6 W, 7 NW, 8 same cell. This is the encoding of the figure facing byte. |
| `0x00404EAD` | `Dist_Manhattan(x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | Returns \|dx\| + \|dy\| and leaves the two absolute deltas in g_absDx and g_absDy. |
| `0x00404F4C` | `Dist_Chebyshev(x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | Returns max(\|dx\|, \|dy\|) and leaves the two absolute deltas in g_absDx and g_absDy. |
| `0x00404E4B` | `Table_Lookup(x, pairs, count, dflt)` | verified | Generic (threshold, value) ladder: returns the value of the first pair whose threshold exceeds x, else the default. Used for the battlefield size class and the men-per-figure scale. |
| `0x0047B8B2` | `Battlefield_BuildFromSkr()` | verified | Loads one 80x80 .skr terrain layer and expands it into the 8-byte-per-cell battlefield, including bridge parts and the two 12-slot deployment marker arrays. See docs/formats/skr.md. |
| `0x0047AAA3` | `Battlefield_BuildRandom()` | inferred | The open-field battlefield generator: writes the same cell array and the same deployment slot arrays as Battlefield_BuildFromSkr, but from the campaign map rather than a .skr layer. |
| `0x0047C4BA` | `Battlefield_BuildCastle(castle)` | inferred | The siege battlefield builder; the third writer of the cell array. |
| `0x0042D806` | `Skr_ReadMap(index, dst)` | verified | Reads one .skr terrain layer: 0x1900 bytes at file offset index * 0x1900 + 0x1674. See docs/formats/skr.md. |
| `0x0048169E` | `Deploy_SlotForUnit(unitOrdinal, side)` | verified | Picks a deployment cell for the n-th unit of a side from the 12-slot marker array: side 0 uses the 0x04 marker slots at 0x00553150, side 4 the 0x0F marker slots at 0x005531B0. | `0x004816F9` | `Deploy_SlotForUnitSiege(unitOrdinal, troopType)` | inferred | The castle-defender placement: maps the troop type to one of the marker slots rather than using the unit ordinal, with a rotating fallback list. |
| `0x00481878` | `Formation_OffsetX(footprint, rows, i)` | verified | Column offset of figure i in a units rectangle: (i / rows) * footprint. |
| `0x00481906` | `Formation_OffsetY(footprint, rows, i, side)` | verified | Row offset of figure i: (i % rows) * footprint, negated for side 0 so both sides face each other. |
| `0x004891AD` | `BattleUnit_Recentre(unit)` | verified | Sets the unit position to the centre of the bounding box of its live figures. |
| `0x00479E90` | `BattleUnit_Order(unit, x, y, attackTarget, fromPlayer, facing)` | verified | Gives a battle unit a destination. Missile units stop short of the target by (range/8 - 3) cells; a unit that mixes missile and melee figures is split into two units. |
| `0x0048143B` | `BattleUnit_Classify(unit)` | verified | Recomputes the unit category from the troop types it still contains: catapult 5, tower 6, ram 7, oil 8, any missile figure 1, otherwise 3. The category selects the order handler. |

| `0x0042BF46` | `Skirmish_FillArmies()` | verified | Copies one row of g_troopsTable into the two army records used by the battle, choosing the side by whether the player is the attacker and the difficulty column per player, then recomputes each army total men (+0x168) and autocalc strength. |
| `0x0042B7F7` | `Skirmish_Setup()` | inferred | Sets up a stand-alone skirmish: army records 1 and 2, loads BATTLES.ENG and the TROOPS table, fills both armies. |

| `0x00497437` | `Order_StopShortOfTarget(x, y, tx, ty, range)` | verified | Pulls a move destination back so the unit halts (range - 3) cells from its target, leaving the result in g_foundTileX / g_foundTileY. This is how missile units stop at firing range instead of closing. |
| `0x0047095E` | `Path_Search(mode, x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | The battlefield pathfinder. A uniform-cost breadth-first flood fill over the 80x80 grid with a circular queue: g_pathCost[cell] holds cost+1 from the start, a cell is expanded only after being reached g_pathStepCost[cell] times (that is how terrain cost is charged), and a step is only allowed between cells whose elevation differs by at most 1. Skipped entirely when the target is adjacent or the straight line is clear. |
| `0x00471718` | `Path_SearchSiege(x, y, tx, ty)` | inferred | The variant of Path_Search used by figures in state 9 - the siege/wall path. |
| `0x00472392` | `Path_Extract(x, y, tx, ty)` | verified | Walks the g_pathCost field downhill from the destination back to the start, writing the route into g_pathWaypoints as (x,y) byte pairs and returning its length. Ties are broken towards the straight-line direction. Returns 0 for no path and 150 for adjacent or over-long. |
| `0x00472875` | `BattleMan_SetPath(man, length)` | verified | Copies `length` waypoints out of g_pathWaypoints into the figure own list at +0x38, sets the count at +0x36 and marks the figure "on route". |
| `0x00491A34` | `BattleMan_NextPathDir(man)` | verified | Returns the direction towards the figure current waypoint and consumes it. The list is walked from the end, because Path_Extract builds it backwards from the destination. |

Used to fix up a move order that lands on unreachable ground. |
| `0x004710F2` | `Path_LineIsClear(x, y, tx, ty)` | inferred | Straight-line passability test; when it succeeds Path_Search does no work at all and the figure walks directly at its target. |
| `0x00471F45` | `Path_BuildBlockedMap()` | inferred | Refreshes the per-cell blocking map the flood fill reads, from the live figure positions. |
| `0x0048B9C1` | `BattleUnit_OrderToEnemyEnd(slot)` | inferred | Sends the current unit to a slot of the opposing sides deployment marker - side 0 to the 0x0F marker, side 4 to the 0x04 marker. | `0x0047ED9B` | `Battle_UpdateHover` | inferred | Runs once per frame from Battle_Frame and recomputes what the pointer is over on the battlefield: clears the five hover globals, asks FUN_004BC222(g_mouseX, g_mouseY) whether the pointer is inside the battlefield viewport and sets g_battleHoverOnField, converts the pointer to a battlefield cell, counts the local player's selected men into DAT_00565404, reads that cell's surface into g_battleHoverSurface and its occupant figure, and then sorts the occupant into g_battleHoverFriendly, g_battleHoverFriendlyPicked or g_battleHoverEnemy depending on whose it is and whether DAT_00553078 says a selection exists. Those globals are what the battle arm of Battle_Frame's cursor ladder reads - but Battle_UpdateHover runs AFTER the cursor is chosen, so the battle pointer is one frame behind the pointer position, the same off-by-one-frame shape docs/screens-county.md section 2.6 records for Screen_FrameInput. |

| `0x00482F86` | `BattleMan_StateNone` | verified | Slot 0 of g_manStateTable: an empty stub, ret. Nothing writes state = 0 anywhere in the struct-typed corpus - BattleMan_StateMelee drops a figure to state 8 rather than 0 - so slot 0 is the array's zero value and not a state the battle uses. |
| `0x00482F91` | `BattleMan_StateDelay` | verified | Slot 1 of g_manStateTable: the wait state. Counts `delay` (+0x30) down and, at zero, restores `dly state` (+0x2F) into `state`, clears walking and moveTick, and re-arms stepFlags bit 0. Fires while it waits if the figure has a weapon class and more than 5 ticks left. Verified two ways: the two fields it drives are the debug panel's own labels `delay` and `dly state` (BattleDebug_Panel), and BattleMan_Step is the writer at the other end - on a blocked friendly it saves the current state into dly state, sets state 1, and sets delay to (otherMan & 1) + 1. |
| `0x004830E9` | `BattleMan_StateDead` | verified | Slot 2 of g_manStateTable. docs/battle.md 2.2 already had state 2 as dead; this is the handler. Steps the collapse animation and counts +0x173 up to 80, then calls BattleMan_Destroy - so a corpse lies on its cell for 80 frames before the slot is freed. Its siege-engine twin is BattleMan_StateEngineDead, which counts to 120. |
| `0x00483A88` | `BattleMan_StateAttackWall` | verified | Slot 6 of g_manStateTable, and it is not merely "blocked". A figure enters it from BattleMan_Step when BattleMan_TryStepDir returns 5, which Cell_TryEnter returns for a cell flagged 0x20 against a non-zero side - the castle wall. The handler plays the strike animation, adds 1 per frame to the rampart counter at 0x00554034 when it stands on surface 5 and to the gate counter at 0x00568DA4 otherwise, converts a rampart cell to surface 4 at 5000, breaches at 20000, and returns to `dly state` once Cell_NeighbourHasSurface finds no more wall to the north. |
| `0x00483FE1` | `BattleMan_StateFillMoat` | verified | Slot 9 of g_manStateTable: the moat fill, as docs/battle-ai.md 5 describes it. The only caller of Anim_Dying. |
| `0x004843BC` | `BattleMan_StateEngineFire` | verified | Slot 12 of g_manStateTable: a catapult aiming and shooting. Seeds the figure's own aim point (+0x28/+0x2A) from the unit's aim point (+0x16/+0x18) when it is still zero - this is the one non-zero writer of that pair that docs/battle.md 2.1 identifies - walks until the aim point is within range >> 3, then counts swingTimer to 100, spawns a missile, steps it eight times on the spot, and wraps the timer at 179. |
| `0x00484A0C` | `BattleMan_StateRamGate` | verified | Slot 14 of g_manStateTable: the siege-engine twin of BattleMan_StateAttackWall, at twenty hits per frame instead of one. Reachable only by a battering ram: BattleMan_TryStepDir sends a siege engine to Cell_TryEnterEngine, whose two leaf tests return 6 - the value BattleMan_Step turns into state 14 - only when troopType == 9. So 1000 frames of ramming breach a gate where a man on foot needs 20000 hits. |
| `0x00484B89` | `BattleMan_StateEngineDead` | verified | Slot 15 of g_manStateTable: the siege-engine death state. Same shape as BattleMan_StateDead with the wreck lying on its cells for 120 frames rather than 80, and the engine animation instead of the collapse. |
| `0x00484BF9` | `BattleMan_StateCloseToAttack` | verified | Slot 17 of g_manStateTable, the state Formation_SendFigure gives a bow or crossbow figure that has something standing on its destination. The largest state handler in the battle at 2340 bytes; it drives the missile record fields directly. |
| `0x00490C59` | `Cell_TryEnterEngine` | verified | The siege-engine arm of BattleMan_TryStepDir: a 3-cell-wide engine tests its whole leading edge rather than one cell. Turns a direction 0..7 into the orthogonal or diagonal leaf test and clamps two cells from the map edge instead of one. |
| `0x00490FA2` | `Cell_TryEnterEngineOrtho` | verified | Tests the three cells of a siege engine's leading edge for an orthogonal step, from the offsets at g_engineEdgeOrtho. Returns Cell_TryEnter's vocabulary plus one value Cell_TryEnter never produces: 6, and only when troopType == 9 - a battering ram against a cell flagged 0x20 or 0x40. That is the sole way into figure state 14. |
| `0x00491147` | `Cell_TryEnterEngineDiag` | verified | The diagonal twin of Cell_TryEnterEngineOrtho, reading g_engineEdgeDiag. Same return vocabulary, same ram-only 6. |
| `0x004861E9` | `Anim_Strike` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_StrikeA2. One of six such thunks at 0x004861E9..0x00486239, each an unconditional `call` to one animation handler; they are the only entry points the state machine uses. |
| `0x004861F9` | `Anim_Walk` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_WalkA2. |
| `0x00486209` | `Anim_Stand` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_StandA2. |
| `0x00486219` | `Anim_Dying` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_DyingA2. |
| `0x00486229` | `Anim_Collapse` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_CollapseA2. |
| `0x00486239` | `Anim_DrawBow` | verified | Sixteen-byte thunk to Anim_DrawBowA2. |
| `0x00486249` | `Anim_StrikeA2` | verified | The strike block of an a2 sheet: frame = dirc2 * N + 6 + g_strikeCycle*[animPhase >> 2] while the figure holds the attacker role (+0x185 == 1), else the standing pose N-1. Phase wraps at 0x27. Called from BattleMan_StateMelee and BattleMan_StateAttackWall. Note this is frames 6.. and NOT the walk cycle - docs/battle.md 13.5 had the two blocks the other way round; see 14.3. |
| `0x004867CC` | `Anim_StrikeA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_StrikeA2: identical code with frames-per-facing 12/13/8/10 replaced by 7/8/5/6, standing pose 11/9/7/9 by 6/5/4/5, two further walk-cycle tables, and horseFrame = dirc rather than dirc * 6. Those six numbers are exactly what the shipped a3 art needs - A3*_swor/mace 69 frames, arch/cros 77, pike 53, psnt 61, all 8*N + 13, and A3_horse.pl8 is 8 frames - so the a2/a3 reading is checked against the art and not only against the code. UNREFERENCED: nothing in the binary calls it, and its address appears in no table. Same for all six a3 handlers. |
| `0x00486D83` | `Anim_WalkA2` | verified | The walk block of an a2 sheet: frame = dirc * N + (animPhase >> 2), phase wrapping at 0x17, so six poses at one per four ticks. Called at the top of BattleMan_StateWalk and BattleMan_StateChase, and in melee only when the figure actually moved. Frames 0..5 are therefore the walk, which is the opposite of what docs/battle.md 13.5 said. |
| `0x0048702F` | `Anim_WalkA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_WalkA2: the same code with N 12/13/8/10 -> 7/8/5/6 and the phase wrapping at 0x0B rather than 0x17, so three poses rather than six. Unreferenced. |
| `0x004872AE` | `Anim_StandA2` | verified | The standing pose of an a2 sheet, and the reader of figure +0x0B/+0x0C: +0x0B counts up each frame and, when it passes +0x0C, resets and turns facingDrawn one step - left on an even map x, right on an odd one. BattleMan_Create seeds +0x0C to ((index*9 + x*16) & 0x3F) + 0xB4, so a standing figure shifts its feet every 180 to 243 frames and no two neighbours do it together. Called by BattleMan_StateIdle, BattleMan_LookForMelee, and by the walk states when the mover reports no progress. |
| `0x004875F7` | `Anim_StandA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_StandA2, N 12/13/8/10 -> 7/8/5/6. Unreferenced. |
| `0x00487908` | `Anim_DyingA2` | verified | The dying block of an a2 sheet: base 8*N + 6 per troop group - 102 macemen and swordsmen, 110 crossbowmen and archers, 70 pikemen, 86 peasants - stepped by animPhase to 0x5F. Its only caller is BattleMan_StateFillMoat. |
| `0x00487AF6` | `Anim_DyingA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_DyingA2, and the arithmetic that pins the whole a2/a3 pairing. Its four bases are 57, 65, 41, 49 = 8*N + 1 for a3's N of 7, 8, 5, 6; the a3 sheets hold 8*N + 13 frames; 13 - 1 = 12 = four half-facings of three dying frames, exactly as in a2. The index space closes with nothing left over for all four troop groups. Unreferenced. |
| `0x00487CE4` | `Anim_CollapseA2` | verified | The six shared frames at 8*N + 0..5 of an a2 sheet - 96, 104, 64, 80 - indexed by the state-2 death timer +0x173 >> 2 and held once it passes 6. Called only by BattleMan_StateDead, so this is a figure falling over; Anim_DyingA2 is the twelve-frame block after it. |
| `0x00487EB1` | `Anim_CollapseA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_CollapseA2: bases 56, 64, 40, 48 = 8*N for a3, and a single frame rather than a six-frame run, which is all a3 has room for. Unreferenced. |
| `0x0048804A` | `Anim_DrawBowA2` | verified | The bow-draw animation: frame = facing * N + curve[swingTimer clamped to 0x4F] + 10, from the 0x50-byte curves g_drawBowCrossbow (troop 1) and g_drawBowArcher (troop 5). The +10 is where the crossbow and archer sheets have room for it: N is 13, frames 0..5 are the walk, 6..8 the strike and 9 the stand, leaving 10..12. Called by BattleMan_FireMissile and BattleMan_StateCloseToAttack. It is also the only function in the battle that reads g_mapRotation, which is the campaign map's orientation; see docs/battle.md 14.5. |
| `0x00488240` | `Anim_DrawBowA3` | verified | The a3 twin of Anim_DrawBowA2: N 13 -> 8, curves g_drawBowCrossbowA3 / g_drawBowArcherA3, base +10 -> +6. Unreferenced. |
| `0x00493CB9` | `Missile_SetupLine(missile, x0, y0, x1, y1)` | verified | Bresenham set-up for a missile's flight: \|dx\| into +0x20, \|dy\| into +0x24, and 2*min - max into the error term at +0x28, then snaps the flight direction +0x2E to the nearer of the eight octants when one axis is more than twice the other. Called by Missile_Spawn. |
| `0x00493B61` | `Missile_StepError` | verified | One Bresenham error update, parameter 1 meaning "the major axis is x". Adds 2*d(minor) when the error is negative and 2*(d(minor) - d(major)) otherwise, and decrements the remaining major-axis count. Called by Missile_Step once per sub-step; g_missileStats' sub-step column is 4 for every class. |
| `0x00494075` | `Missile_OffMap` | verified | Returns 1 when the missile's cell coordinates (+0x14, +0x16) have left 0..79 in either axis. The battlefield bound 0x50 again, so a missile that misses everything dies at the map edge rather than flying forever. |
| `0x004941C5` | `Missile_StepTowardTargetX` | verified | Moves the missile's x (+0x0A, in 1/32 cell) one unit toward its target x (+0x0E), or leaves it alone when they are equal. |
| `0x00494125` | `Missile_StepTowardTargetY` | verified | Moves the missile's y (+0x0C) one unit toward its target y (+0x10). The twin of Missile_StepTowardTargetX. |
| `0x0046EFBE` | `Missile_LinkToCell(missile)` | verified | Pushes a missile onto the list of missiles standing on its cell: cell byte +6 is the head, missile +0x04 is the next link, and the walk gives up after ten so a corrupt list cannot hang the frame. Confirms docs/battle.md 3's reading of cell byte +6. |
| `0x0046EF3C` | `Missile_ClearCellLists` | verified | Sweeps missiles 1..100 and clears both ends of the per-cell missile list - cell byte +6 and the missile's own next link. The loop bound is i < 0x65, the same 100 as Missile_UpdateAll. |
| `0x00496F72` | `Cell_NeighbourHasSurface(x, y, cellOffset, surface)` | verified | Tests the four orthogonal neighbours of a cell for a given surface value (cell byte +7), clipped at the map edge. Verified by arithmetic: the four bases it reads are 0x00543E67, 0x00544367, 0x005440DF and 0x005440EF, which are g_battlefield + 7 offset by -0x280, +0x280, -8 and +8 - one row north, one row south, one cell west, one cell east. |
| `0x0046F525` | `Dir_FromDeltaOctant(x0, y0, x1, y1)` | verified | A second direction-from-delta, snapping to the eight compass points by a 3:1 ratio test rather than exactly: a delta within a third of an axis is treated as on that axis. Same numbering as Dir_FromDelta - 0 north, 2 east, 4 south, 6 west - which is a third independent statement of the facing table. Used by BattleMan_StateEngineFire to aim a catapult. |
| `0x00477DFC` | `Battle_CheckOutcome()` | verified | The per-frame test for whether the battle is over - the half of the battle model that decides anything, and previously in neither symbols.json nor hypotheses.json. A FIELD battle ends exactly two ways: one side's living-men counter (g_battleMenA, g_battleMenB, both drawn on the battle HUD) reaching zero, or g_battleWithdrawal marking a withdrawal, which is tested FIRST and so outranks annihilation. There is no morale break, no rout threshold and no clock. Three further arms are sieges: the escape-tile flag DAT_00553F3C, and with no breach and no engines left either 'assault repulsed, repeat' (breach and approach scores reset to 4 when the castle level is under 3) or the besieger loses. It then raises the outcome screen 0x2B, and once DAT_00568470 counts past 5000 it calls Battle_WriteBackCasualties and Battle_ReturnToCampaign(1). |
| `0x00478419` | `Battle_SelectOutcomeBanner()` | verified | Chooses which of Screen_BattleOutcome's seven L2.eng group 82 heading/body pairs to draw, from two questions: was it a siege, and is g_localPlayer the winner's owner (g_battleWinnerOwner). Field: 0 won, 1 lost. Siege with the local player winning: 2 if A won (the player besieged and took the castle), 4 if B won (the player held it and the siege was lifted). Siege with the local player losing: 5 if A won (the player's castle fell), 3 if B won (the player was driven off). Each of the four siege arms reads as a different sentence, which is the check on the mapping - A is always the besieger and B always the garrison. Independently confirms that g_battleLoser holds the winner. |
| `0x0047F474` | `Battle_WriteBackCasualties()` | verified | How a fought battle's casualties reach the campaign. Zeroes both army records' +0x168, +0x196 and all eleven +0x16C counts, then walks the 80 figures of g_battleMen and adds each living figure's men into its owner's record - into +0x16C + troopType*2 for a levied figure and into +0x196 for a mercenary one, with +0x168 accumulated alongside. Only troop types 0..6 are counted, so the four battle-only slots are zeroed and never rebuilt: siege engines do not survive a battle. Called immediately before every Battle_ReturnToCampaign(1). |
| `0x0043B622` | `Battle_Decline()` | verified | Answering 'no' to the prompt screen's 'Will you take the field?'. Guarded on g_screenId == 0x12 so it is idempotent, it runs Battle_AutoResolve, Battle_ReturnToCampaign(0) and raises the report screen 0x13. So DECLINING A BATTLE IS EXACTLY THE AUTOCALC - a way out of watching the battle, not out of fighting it. The multiplayer answer timeout and an end-of-turn sync both route here as an auto-decline; in single player the prompt waits forever. |
| `0x004784CA` | `Siege_RecordCastleDamage` | verified | The end-of-siege bookkeeper, called from Battle_ReturnToCampaign's siege arm, and the ONLY writer of county +0x1C3 to 2. Returns unless g_battleIsSiege and one of the two damage accumulators is non-zero. Stores the battle's breach and approach scores into +0x1E4..+0x1F1 (which Castle_BuildTick zeroes on completion), then bills the repair: castleLevel < 2 charges wood +0x1D4/+0x1E0 = wallDamage*10, otherwise stone +0x1D0/+0x1DC = wallDamage*15, and work +0x1CC/+0x1D8 = breachDamage*5 + wallDamage*15 - ADDING to whatever a build already under way had owed. So a wooden castle is repaired in wood and a stone one in stone. Finally +0x1F9 = g_castleLevel, castleDegraded = 2, +0x1C4 = 0. |

| `0x00554480` | `g_battleMen` | verified | Battle figure array, 81 records of 0x1B0 bytes, index 1..80 (0 unused). Full map in docs/battle.md. Eighty usable slots but eighty-one records of storage: every sweep is for (i = 1; i < 0x51; i++), so index 80 is live, and nothing else in the binary claims the space behind it. |
