[V] The ladder in Wages_PayAll is six seasons long: stage 0 warns or loses the mercenaries, 1-3 desert men, 4 deserts men and warns of mutiny, 5 destroys everything and resets the counter to 0. Group 271 says "it has been over a year since your men received any wages" at stage 4, and stages 1 to 4 are exactly four seasons. |
| `0x004AC185` | `County_RaiseRevolt(county)` | verified | What unrest 4 does. Finds a free road tile, or failing that a free open tile, spawns a kind-2 unit - revolting peasants - of 30% of the county's population at morale 50 with no shield, takes those people out of the population, and calls County_MakeIndependent. Returns 0 and leaves the unrest counter alone if there is nowhere to put the mob, so a county with no free tile can sit at unrest 4 indefinitely. All 30% land in troops[0], the peasant slot. |
| `0x00450639` | `Labour_ToggleShare(county, share, on, divisor)` | verified | Switches one of the three farm labour shares at county +0x130 in or out and renormalises the group to 100. Turning one on gives it g_shareTable[n] / divisor, where n is how many of the three are currently non-zero, and scales the others by 100 minus that; turning it off scales the survivors up by 100 / (100 - its share). Either way the rounding residue is dropped on whichever of the three is largest, defaulting to share 1. Its only caller is Field_SetType, which passes share 2 - field reclamation - and turns it on exactly when county +0x204 is non-zero, so painting a field is what puts reclamation into the labour split. [V] Established by the field-brush work; restated here because the name was living in Rust doc comments and docs/kingdom.md only. The five-member industry twin is Labour_ToggleIndustryShare. |
| `0x004502CA` | `Labour_ToggleIndustryShare(county, share, on)` | verified | The five-member twin of Labour_ToggleShare, over labourShare[3..7] - castle, iron, stone, wood, blacksmith - with no divisor and its residue defaulting to share 6, wood. [V] The two functions are what settle which index is which job, because each ends by forcing its own group to sum to 100: this one computes 100 minus shares 6, 5, 4, 7 and 3, and Labour_ToggleShare computes 100 minus shares 0, 1 and 2. The groups are therefore {0,1,2} and {3,4,5,6,7} exactly, and the England turn-one fixture then reads them off - every county carries farm {33, 50, 17} and every unowned county carries industry {0,0,0,100,0} with all its spare people idle, which is the wood share pointing at a job whose ceiling is 0. So labourShare is grain, cattle, reclamation, castle, iron, stone, wood, blacksmith, and it is Idle Townsfolk that has no entry - docs/records.json says grain has none, and that is the wrong end. |
| `0x00469B8D` | `County_RecountFields(county)` | verified | Rebuilds the county's five field tallies from the map: walks its 20 slots in g_countyFieldTiles and buckets each tile's graphic byte out of g_tiles - 1 fallow (+0x1FF), 2..14 grain (+0x201), 15..22 pasture (+0x200), 0/23/24 to +0x203 and 25 and above to +0x204. The tallies are a cache; the map is the state. +0x204 is load-bearing: Field_SetType switches the reclamation labour share on exactly when it is non-zero. [V] Recomputed from g_countyFieldTiles and g_tiles in the England turn-one fixture, all five tallies reproduce in all fourteen counties - 168 field tiles - which pins every band boundary. Independently reproduced by the field-brush work on every other reachable save including the four-county battle map. It is also the first call in County_RefreshEstimates, so the herd's crowding estimate always runs against a field count refreshed one call earlier. |
| `0x0049B42B` | `Realm_RecountStrength(realm)` | verified | Rebuilds realm +0x04 as 3 * owned counties + 1 * armies (g_units slots 1..150 with type 1; merchants, transports and mobs do not count) and, if that comes out zero, the realm has just been eliminated. [V] The whole body is guarded on +0x04 already being non-zero, so it fires exactly once per realm. The message is chosen on "is this the local player", not on "is this a human": g_localPlayer gets L2.eng group 224 "Defeat!" addressed from and to itself, an AI raises group 194 "Foiled again." from itself, and a second human in a network game is told nothing at all - the voice rotation at +0x159 advances in all three cases. Then Score_RankRealms, and in multiplayer Net_SendCommand(0x24) if it was me. Four callers: AI_RunTurnStep's step 0 (EVERY realm, the human included), County_ChangeOwner - which calls it on the outgoing owner BEFORE writing the new one, so that call can never eliminate anybody - and the two arms of the post-battle resolution. |
| `0x0049B6D3` | `Turn_BeginPlayersTurn()` | verified | Resets every realm's AI program counter at the top of phase 4: aiStep = 0, or 999 for a realm at zero strength, and g_realmsActive counts the rest. [V] The counter is reset for the human too, because AI_RunTurnStep's step 0 runs for every realm. |
| `0x0044C6C4` | `Field_OrderReclamation(county, want)` | verified | Orders want of a county twenty field tiles put under reclamation: walks them in slot order, paints terrain 0x19 on a wasteland tile (terrain 0) and skips a tile already in use. [V] A tile already reclaiming (terrain >= 0x19) is NOT repainted but still spends a place in the quota - the goto skips the decrement for an in-use field and falls through to it for a reclaiming one - so a county told to add one while one is already under way adds nothing. This is what the AI field ladder in Ai_ManageCountyFarms and AI_ManageFields actually calls. |
| `0x0044015A` | `Sync_RecordDigest(base, size, skip)` | verified | The multiplayer state checksum's kernel: an 8-bit truncating sum of every byte of one record from offset `skip` to `size`. [V] Its six (size, skip) call sites - (0x300,5) counties, (0x160,6) realms, (0x1A4,0) units, (0x1B0,0x12) battle men, (0x4C,4) missiles, (0x34,0) battle units - are byte-for-byte the six descriptors in g_syncBlocks (0x004D5B10) read out of the exe. So the original's digest is a whole-record hash minus a fixed prefix, not a field list; see docs/formats/eng.md 5.2 and correction C30. |
| `0x00440231` | `Sync_BuildDigest()` | verified | Fills the local player's 10-byte g_syncDigest record: byte 1 counties, 2 realms, 3 units in the campaign phase; 5 battle men, 6 missiles, 7 battle units in battle; byte 0 the sum of bytes 1..8 and byte 9 a (tick & 0x7F) + 1 frame tag. [V] L2.eng group 14 indices 2..9 name those eight slots COUNTY PLAYER PIECE 'CTY MAP' FIGURE ARROW 'AI GROUP' 'BAT MAP', and the two slots this function never writes - 4 and 8 - are exactly the two the strings call maps. Its last statement before the total overwrites byte 7 with the constant 1, so the battle-unit block's divergences are silenced in the shipped build. |
| `0x00440DD2` | `Sync_BlockAgrees(block)` | verified | Compares byte `block` of g_syncDigest across every live human realm. [V] Returns 2 - wait, not divergence - unless every peer's byte 9 frame tag is equal and non-zero; 0 and sets a per-block flag in the ten-entry array at 0x0053F090 on a mismatch; 1 on agreement. The flag array is one entry per L2.eng group 14 block name. |
| `0x0043F939` | `Sync_ResendState()` | verified | The full-state resync L2.eng group 14 index 16 calls 'The master machine will resync the game.' [V] Sends the current snapshot as 400 net commands of 200 bytes - 80,000 bytes - then calls Sync_Rollback. |
| `0x0044098F` | `Sync_DrawRecordDigests()` | verified | Debug overlay: per-record Sync_RecordDigest values for 16 counties, 5 realms and 24 units, using the inline literals "cty ", "plyr " and "pce ". [V] It is the shipped replacement for L2.eng group 14's COUNTY/PLAYER/PIECE labels, which is why nothing in the binary draws that group. Gated on DAT_0056D690 and a non-zero divergence timestamp. |
| `0x0043F5C5` | `Sync_Rollback()` | verified | Multiplayer divergence recovery, promoted from hypotheses.json where it was Battle_ReplayFrame with the caveat 'whether it is a replay, a demo loop or a network resync was NOT established'. [V] It is the network resync: FUN_00440666 calls it on the branch where Sync_BlockAgrees(0) reports a divergence, and Sync_ResendState calls it after pushing the whole snapshot. It restores the last agreed snapshot - g_battleMen, g_missiles, g_battleUnits and both PRNG states - bumps the 'divergances' counter the shipped debug overlay draws, and after four consecutive failures reloads lastturn.sva/.svb and posts L2.eng group 259, 'Com-link error.' See docs/formats/eng.md 5.2. |
| `0x0049BD99` | `Game_SetupRealmsAndCounties()` | verified | The starting position, from Game_NewGame after Map_InitScenario. Every county takes grain, herd, population, health and happiness from g_countyStatus[g_startCountyStatus]; every realm with a lord takes its start county out of g_playerStartTable, gold from g_startingGoldChosen, 50 each of iron, wood and stone, weapons from g_startArmoury[g_startWeapons] with difficulty * 20 extra in slot 4 for an AI, a garrison raised by Army_Create from g_startTroops[g_startArmySize], and the castle from g_startCastle. A realm with no lord is skipped entirely. Finally every unowned county gets 100 more grain. |
| `0x00436D02` | `Castle_Order(county, level)` | verified | THE ONLY WRITER of county +0x1C3 to 1, and the whole castle-ordering rule. level is 0-based. If castleType != 0 it copies castleType into castleBuilding (so +0x1C1 is the castle that WAS there, not the one being built) and subtracts g_castleMaterial[castleType-1] from the new cost, refunding a negative difference into the realm. Then castleType = level+1 IMMEDIATELY, castleRuined = 0, castleDegraded = 1, +0x1B0 = 1, +0x1CC = +0x1D8 = g_castleWorkforce[level], +0x1DC = stone total, +0x1E0 = wood total, +0x1C4 = 0; Castle_StampTile, Castle_EvictTile, Labour_ToggleIndustryShare(county, 3, 1); then it takes what the realm HAS and leaves the rest owing in +0x1D0 (stone) and +0x1D4 (wood). THERE IS NO AFFORDABILITY GUARD. |
| `0x00450CCD` | `Castle_DeliverMaterials(county)` | verified | The season's cart of wood and stone, called first thing in Castle_BuildTick's degraded branch. Takes as much of county +0x1D4 (wood owed) and +0x1D0 (stone owed) out of the realm's stores as they hold and leaves the remainder owing. THE MATERIALS ARE DRAWN DOWN OVER SEASONS, NOT PAID UP FRONT - Castle_Order takes only what the realm had at the moment of the order. |
| `0x00450FB4` | `Castle_MaterialsPercent(county)` | verified | min(100 - Pct(woodOwed, woodTotal), 100 - Pct(stoneOwed, stoneTotal)), or 0 when castleDegraded is 0. The gate on all castle work: Castle_BuildTick spends no labour and Castle_BuildEstimate gives a ceiling of 0 until this is above 99. It is whole-percent integer arithmetic, so a build a fraction of a percent short of its bill reads as fully delivered. |
| `0x004AA90A` | `Levy_Seed` | verified | The PLAYER's levy-basket seeding; Levy_Init (0x004AAA80) is the AI's. Byte for byte the same seeding plus three UI resets: g_armourySelectedType = 0, the walking-soldier flag DAT_005679D0 = 0, and _DAT_0057C8E4 = 0. Zeroes all eight slots' chosen and available, sets slots 1..6 available and remaining from realm +0x140 + (t-1)*4, and puts the levied headcount in slot 0 and slot 7. Reads the realm out of county +0x05, the owner, rather than taking it as an argument. [V] Called by Sidebar_Button, by RaiseArmy_Continue and by the 0x17 right-release arm - every door into the armoury. |

Realms_AssignLords hands out at most this many lords and writes strength = 0 into every realm past it, so it is what decides how many realms exist. One of only seven globals in the 0x0053F2xx block that Save_Write stores. |
| `0x0053F270` | `g_startWeapons` | verified | Row of g_startArmoury the game starts on, 0..3 - the committed Weapons option. Campaign maps take it from the table's +0x14 column, which is 1 on every row of both tracks. Not saved. |
| `0x0053F274` | `g_startArmySize` | verified | Row of g_startTroops the starting garrison is raised from, 0..3 - the committed Army Size option; 0 raises no army at all, which is the test Game_SetupRealmsAndCounties makes. Campaign maps take it from +0x18, which is 0 on every row of both tracks, so a campaign never starts you with an army. Not saved. |
| `0x0053F278` | `g_startingGoldChosen` | verified | The starting treasury in crowns - g_startingGold[Crowns] from Setup_CommitOptions, or the campaign table's +0x08 column, which is where docs/kingdom.md 8.5's 5000 / 2500 / 1000 comes from. Written into every realm's gold. Not saved. |
| `0x0053F27C` | `g_startCountyStatus` | verified | Row of g_countyStatus every county starts on, 0..2 - the committed County Status option, weak / medium / strong. Campaign maps take it from +0x10. Not saved. |
| `0x0053F280` | `g_startCastle` | verified | The castle type every start county is given, 0..5 - none, wooden, bailey, keep, stone, royal - the committed Starting Castle option, or the campaign table's +0x0C column, which runs 1, 1, 2, 3, 4, 4, 5, 5 up track A. An earlier reading of that column as the opponent count was wrong: the opponent count is +0x1C. Not saved. |
| `0x004DBBF8` | `g_timeLimitSeconds` | verified | Seven i32 the Time limit drop-down indexes: 30, 60, 120, 240, 480, 600, 0. Seven values for L2.eng group 103's seven strings "30 secs" .. "no limit", and the last is 0 rather than a sentinel of its own. |
| `0x004DBC18` | `g_startingGold` | verified | Five i32 the Crowns drop-down indexes: 100, 500, 1000, 2500, 5000 - literally the five strings. Also the source of the campaign table's purse column. |
| `0x004DC0D0` | `g_countyStatus` | verified | Three rows of five i32, stride 0x14, the County Status option indexes: {grain, herd, population, healthMeter, happiness} = {10, 40, 167, 45, 41} weak, {0, 95, 417, 65, 65} medium, {500, 330, 1181, 85, 85} strong. Medium really does start with no grain. It sits exactly between g_startArmoury (which ends at 0x004DC0D0) and g_startTroops (0x004DC110), three tables closing with no overlap and a four-byte gap. |

<!-- END symbols.json: kingdom -->

## Interface and screen layout

The presentation layer: the campaign screen's viewport, its two zooms, its chrome and its
minimap. Written up in full in [`screens.md`](screens.md).

This section did not exist until this work, and its absence caused a real error:
`crates/l2-view` drew the whole 64 × 64 map at once, which is a view the original does not
have. `Map_SetZoom` is the anchor — ten globals per zoom, and its derived width comes out
at 480 in every case, which is what fixes the 160-pixel right column.

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: ui -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x00451BBA` | `Minimap_ComputeBands()` | verified | Recomputes the three minimap overlay ratings for every county, and Minimap_DrawOverlay calls it first on every draw. It is the ONLY writer of county +0x01, +0x02 and +0x03 - three of the five bytes Sync_CompareState skips, which is what they are: interface state, not simulation. +0x03 labour: 0 if job 0 or 1 is below its wanted floor, else 6 if idle townsfolk plus over-staffed jobs is zero, else 5. +0x02 food: 0 if rationAchieved < rationWanted, else 6. +0x01 happiness: happiness / 20, so 0..5. TWO OF THE THREE MODES ROUTINELY PRODUCE 6, which is off the end of the six-entry g_minimapRatingRamp and colours nothing - so the shipped food overlay is a single red mark on counties that went short and nothing else, and the labour overlay paints only the ramp's two ends. That is not a misreading: a second food branch spreading rationAchieved over bands 1..5 sits behind DAT_00553E60, a debug flag zeroed at 0x00497500 and toggled only in the window procedure at 0x004B29BE. Found by scanning the binary for divide-by-20 sites; there are exactly two. docs/decisions.md C59. |
| `0x00451FCC` | `Map_SetZoom(zoom)` | verified | The only writer of the campaign map geometry. Sets ten globals per zoom: g_mapZoom, g_mapScrollStep 1/2/4, g_mapViewCols 8/17/40, g_mapViewRows 30/64/128, g_mapViewX 0/4/0, g_mapViewY 9/17/21, g_mapTilePitch 60/28/12, g_mapHalfPitch and g_mapTileHalfStep 30/14/6, g_mapRowStep 15/7/3; then derives g_mapViewRight = pitch*cols + viewX (480 at all three) and g_mapViewBottom = (rows+1)*rowStep + viewY (474/472/408). Case 1 is unreachable: nothing in the binary ever passes 1, and g_mapZoom has no other writer than 0. |
| `0x00429B1D` | `Map_ClampScroll()` | verified | Clamps g_mapStartCol to 0..0x40-g_mapViewCols and g_mapStartRow to 0..0x80-g_mapViewRows. At the far zoom that is col <= 24 and row <= 0, i.e. no vertical scroll at all. |
| `0x00429BA4` | `Map_PickTile(mode)` | verified | Screen -> lattice hit test for the campaign map. Rejects x outside [g_mapViewX, g_mapViewX + pitch*cols) and y outside [viewY + rowStep, viewY + rowStep*(rows+1)), then divides by g_mapTileHalfStep and g_mapRowStep and resolves the diamond with a parity and remainder test. Returns 0 when the cell is off-map. |
| `0x004298C1` | `Map_BuildLattice(rotation)` | verified | Clears g_screenLattice, reloads the tail with Map_LoadLattice, then projects all 64x64 tiles into it for rotation 0/2/4/6. Rotation 0 is row = 1+y+x, col = (64-y+x)/2, value = (64*y+x)*8 - exactly the mapping docs/formats/maps-layers.md section 4 verifies against a live process. |
| `0x00429F12` | `Map_RotateCW()` | verified | g_mapRotation += 2 (mod 8), rebuilds the lattice and re-projects the scroll origin into the new orientation. |
| `0x0042A01B` | `Map_RotateCCW()` | verified | g_mapRotation -= 2 (mod 8), the inverse of Map_RotateCW. |
| `0x0043278B` | `Map_CentreOnTile(tileOffset)` | verified | Scans the 129x65 lattice for the cell holding tileOffset and, only at zoom 0, sets g_mapStartCol = col-4 and g_mapStartRow = (row & ~1) - 12. The row is forced even because the render walk alternates aligned and half-offset lattice rows from the origin. |
| `0x00434FA1` | `Map_ToggleZoom()` | verified | The campaign zoom button: 0 -> Map_ZoomOut, 2 -> Map_ZoomIn. There are only two campaign zooms. |
| `0x00434FD5` | `Map_ZoomOut()` | verified | Saves the scroll origin, sets col 0x0E row 0x0C, Map_SetZoom(2), clamps, reloads the 10x6 tile sets through Gfx_LoadCountyMode and plays S033_02.wav. |
| `0x0043505A` | `Map_ZoomIn()` | verified | Restores the saved scroll origin, Map_SetZoom(0), clamps and reloads the 58x30 tile sets. |
| `0x004350A1` | `Map_ZoomInAtTile()` | verified | Zooms in centred on the last picked tile, using the same col-4 / (row & ~1)-12 centring as Map_CentreOnTile. |
| `0x00431F59` | `Map_ScrollStep()` | verified | Applies g_mapScrollDir 0..7 (N, NE, E, SE, S, SW, W, NW) to the scroll origin: row moves by +/-2*g_mapScrollStep and col by +/-g_mapScrollStep, which is one map tile per step. Reverts the move when Map_ScrollThrottle says it is too soon. In battle it moves the battle view origin instead. |
| `0x00432221` | `Map_EdgeScroll()` | verified | Turns the desktop cursor position into g_mapScrollDir: an edge is the outermost pixel of the desktop (x == 0, x == GetSystemMetrics(0)-1, likewise for y), and 8 means no edge. Returns 0 without scrolling when the campaign map is at the far zoom, so the far view never moves. |
| `0x004BBBE3` | `Map_ScrollThrottle()` | verified | Rate limit for Map_ScrollStep from the Scroll Speed option: one step every ((100 - g_optScrollSpeed)/10)*12 + 2 ms, and never when that quotient reaches 10 - so a speed of 0 disables scrolling. [V] The clock is timeGetTime (WINMM IAT 0x005CF5C4), not GetTickCount, against g_lastScrollTick; a negative elapsed is treated as 10000 ms, which is the wrap guard. [V] g_screenId == 0x10 adds 2 to the quotient, i.e. +24 ms. It is this function's only call site, and Map_EdgeScroll's five call sites are unconditional every frame - so the pointer-at-edge detection runs at frame rate and only the movement is gated. The remainder is discarded rather than accumulated. docs/decisions.md C59. |
| `0x004AE310` | `Options_SetDefaults()` | verified | Fills g_options with the shipped defaults: the magic 0x7EC at g_optionsMagic, the 31-byte player name 'Player1' (string at 0x004DF17C), g_optGameSpeed = 90, g_optScrollSpeed = 60, and a run of about twelve flags set to 1. Two callers: 0x004AE228 unconditionally, and 0x004AE2D3 guarded by a comparison of g_optionsMagic against 0x7EC - [I] first run or a settings file from another build. It was unnamed until docs/decisions.md C59 needed the scroll speed's default. |
| `0x0043253A` | `Minimap_Click()` | verified | Campaign minimap click. Accepts x in 480..607 and y in 25..152, reads g_minimapCounty[(x-480) + (y-25)*128] and, for a non-zero county, selects it and calls Map_CentreOnTile. That 128x128 rectangle is exactly the raster Minimap_Load filled. |
| `0x00432443` | `BattleMap_Click()` | verified | Battle overview click. Accepts x in 480..639 and y in 24..183 and maps it to a battlefield cell by (x-480)/2, (y-24)/2 - a 160x160 panel at 2 pixels per cell over an 80x80 battlefield. |
| `0x00432640` | `Screen_HitRegion()` | verified | Classifies the cursor: region 1 is y < 24 (the menu bar), region 2 is the overview panel rectangle g_overviewX/W by g_overviewY/H, whose top is raised by 24 in campaign mode - so campaign region 2 is x 480..639, y 24..207. |
| `0x0040F5FD` | `Screen_DrawCampaign(full)` | verified | Draws the whole campaign screen: Misc_cty frames 54, 57 and 59 at x 478 (y 24, 430, 460), the map through Map_DrawFrame, the menu bar, the county panel, the minimap at (480, 25), and finally Palette_Set(g_paletteBase01). |
| `0x00419C78` | `Screen_DrawMenuBar()` | verified | The 640x24 bar at y 0: background tiled from Panels.pl8 frames 196+(c mod 8) at 24px steps (25 cells from x 0 plus 2 from x 592 = 640 exactly), a bevel outline, the File/Options/Help titles from L2.eng groups 1/2/3, one 13x16 Misc_cty banner per live realm at x 270+16i y 4, the year and season at x 360 and the local realm treasury at x 500. |
| `0x00410AA9` | `Minimap_Draw(x, y)` | verified | Called as Minimap_Draw(0x1E0, 0x19). Blits the 128x128 g_minimapPixels raster at (x-2, y+3) = (478, 28), tints it through Minimap_DrawOverlay, draws a 29x123 Misc_cty strip at (x+0x83, y+7) and a mode badge at (x+5, y+5). The picture is drawn at (478,28) while Minimap_Click hit-tests (480,25): that 2/3 pixel disagreement is the original. |
| `0x00410CBD` | `Minimap_DrawOverlay(selected, x, y, mode)` | verified | Recolours the minimap raster per county. Source pixels 10..13 are land inside a county and are replaced from an 8-byte-per-realm ramp at g_minimapRealmRamp indexed realmColour*8 + (v-10); shade 10 of the selected county becomes index 0x20. Modes 1..3 instead colour the county from the 6-entry g_minimapRatingRamp, and ALL THREE rating branches skip the pixel unless owner == g_localPlayer - the statistic modes show your own counties and nobody else's. Everything else is left as the picture drew it. CORRECTED: the rating index is county +0x03, +0x02 and +0x01, not +0xB3/+0xB2/+0xB1 - the old numbers were 0x0053F9B3 read as an offset rather than as an address into g_counties (0x0053F9B0). Minimap_ComputeBands (0x00451BBA) fills those three bytes and this function calls it first on every draw. docs/decisions.md C59. |
| `0x0043AB76` | `Minimap_ModeButton()` | verified | Acts on g_uiHotspotId. THE FOUR BUTTONS ARE NOT RADIO BUTTONS, and this entry used to say they were. In mode 0: 1..3 set g_minimapMode and 4 calls Map_ToggleZoom. In any other mode: 4 turns the overlay off and 1..3 do nothing at all - so food to happiness takes three clicks. The artwork says the same thing from the other side: Misc_cty frame 0x5C, drawn in mode 0, carries four buttons and frame 0x5B, drawn while a mode is up, carries a colour bar and one. docs/decisions.md C59. |
| `0x0041A734` | `Screen_DrawEndTurn(force)` | verified | Draws Misc_cty frame 59 (162x20) at (478, 460) and centres L2.eng group 4, "End turn", in it at (478, 462) in the 9-point font. That strip is the End Turn button. |
| `0x00409934` | `Ui_DrawBoxBorder(style, x, y, cols, rows)` | verified | Draws a 16-pixel-cell border from Panels.pl8: frames 0/1/2/3 are the TL/TR/BR/BL corners, 4..15 the top edge, 16..27 the bottom, 28..39 the left and 40..51 the right, each cycled (n-1) mod 12. A style above 0 adds 0xCC to reach the second complete border set at 204..255; style 2 also replaces the two top corners with edge pieces and omits the top edge. |
| `0x00409C93` | `Ui_DrawBoxInterior(x, y, cols, rows)` | verified | Fills a 16-pixel-cell area with Panels.pl8 frames 52 + (c mod 12) + (r mod 12)*12 - a 144-frame texture that the file lays out as a 12x12 grid on the artists sheet. |
| `0x00409397` | `Ui_DrawBox(x, y, cols, rows)` | verified | Ui_DrawBoxBorder(0, ...) plus Ui_DrawBoxInterior inset by one cell. Sizes are in 16-pixel cells: the strip under the far-zoom map is Ui_DrawBox(0, 412, 30, 4) = 480x64. |
| `0x00409E09` | `Ui_DrawTileStrip(x, y, cols, rows)` | verified | Tiles Panels.pl8 frames 196 + (c mod 8) - the eight 24x24 frames - at 24-pixel steps. The menu bar background. |
| `0x0040A567` | `Pl8_DrawFrameHere(sheet, frame, x, y)` | verified | Pl8_DrawFrame without the inline range diagnostics: reads the 16-byte frame record, sets g_drawX/g_drawY and g_clipDstAdvance = 0x280 - width. That 0x280 is where the 640-byte screen stride is visible. |
| `0x0040A01D` | `Blit_Raster(src, x, y, w, h)` | verified | Blits a bare w*h raster with no PL8 record through the standard clipper. Used for the 128x128 minimap picture. |
| `0x00403FDD` | `Ui_DrawBevelRect(x, y, w, h)` | verified | Four clipped lines: top and right in palette index 0x1F, bottom and left in 0x10. |
| `0x004B0AB5` | `Palette_Set(pal)` | verified | Copies 256 six-bit RGB triples into the display palette, widening by multiplying by 4, then forces entry 0 to black. |
| `0x004984DC` | `Gfx_LoadCountyMode()` | verified | Loads the campaign map artwork. base = (g_mapZoom == 2) ? 0x20 : 0, plus (g_season-1)*8; then eight consecutive g_resourceTable entries go to g_bankBase, g_bankMtns, g_bankRoads, g_bankTown, g_bankCastle, g_spriteSheetA, g_spriteSheetB and g_flagsSheet in that order - independent confirmation of the bank order in maps-layers.md section 1.1. Misc_cty.pl8 follows into g_miscCtySheet. |
| `0x004050F6` | `Map_DrawFrame()` | verified | One campaign map frame. Zeroes g_countyTileTally, gives the current selection a 30-tile head start, runs Map_RenderIso (which tallies +1 per tile drawn and +5 for a castle tile), then the flag and army passes, and finally - only at zoom 0 - moves g_selectedCounty to whichever county filled most of the viewport. Scrolling therefore changes which county the right panel describes. |
| `0x00405C2F` | `Map_RenderOffsetRow()` | verified | One half-offset lattice row: the first tile with clip mode 3 (right half, origin x - halfPitch), then cols-1 tiles a half pitch to the right, then one with mode 4 (left half). Consumes cols+1 lattice columns. |
| `0x00405AE9` | `Map_RenderAlignedRow()` | verified | One aligned lattice row: cols tiles at x = g_mapViewX + c*pitch, clip mode 0, tallying each into g_countyTileTally. |
| `0x0042A7F1` | `Map_DrawSurroundTile(x, y, mode)` | verified | Draws an off-map surround cell: base bank, frame = the lattice cell value minus 0x0FFF0000, which is the background byte the map file stores in its 65x129 tail. |
| `0x004081A6` | `Map_DrawPathMarker(mode)` | verified | The gold path-preview ball, NOT the county flag. Draws Flags1a.pl8 frame 0x38 + cost on every runtime tile carrying **bank** bit 0x40 - the transient mark Path_MarkPreviewTiles (0x004A91BA) sets along the local player's ordered path and FUN_0046C6BA clears grid-wide - at offset (+0x14, +6) from the tile, clipped by Clip_Horizontal(g_mapViewX, 0x1DE) and Clip_Vertical(0x18, 0x1DA), so the map viewport is x < 478 and y in 24..473. The cost comes from g_moveDistLocal minus one, clamped to 0 when the step is out of the unit's remaining allowance, and a castle, settlement or plot draws frame 0x4E instead. CORRECTED: this entry said "county flag over a castle tile" and was marked verified; four documents carried the same error. The county and castle flags are FUN_004071A0, off plane-0 bank bit 0x80, frame (shield - 1) * 8 + phase. The two bits are both called 0x40 and 0x80 in different planes, which is how the readings were crossed. docs/decisions.md C49, and docs/armies.md 2.3 has used this name all along. |
| `0x00408438` | `Map_DrawArmies(mode)` | verified | Walks the unit list hanging off a tile and draws each from g_spriteSheetA or g_spriteSheetB, offset by an 8-orientation by 16-frame table per zoom at 0x004D8108/0x004D8188 (zoom 0), 0x004D8208/0x004D8288 (zoom 1) and 0x004D8308/0x004D8388 (zoom 2). The sprite direction is the unit facing minus g_mapRotation, mod 8. |
| `0x00406673` | `Map_DrawTileApex(a, b, mode)` | verified | Draws the chevron ("overhang") records that sit above a diamond tile. Reaches them by adding a fixed body size to the frame data pointer: 900 at zoom 0, 0xC4 at zoom 1, 0x24 at zoom 2, which are h*h/2 for h = 30, 14 and 6 and so pin the three tile sizes from the instruction stream alone. |
| `0x0040C5B0` | `Ui_DrawMenuTitles(items, count)` | verified | Lays the menu-bar titles out left to right from a 16-byte-per-item table, writing each measured x back into the table so the drop-downs know where to open. The campaign table is g_menuBarItems, three items, L2.eng groups 1, 2 and 3. |
| `0x00498270` | `Map_InitMode()` | verified | Campaign map bring-up: scroll origin row 0x4A col 0x14, zoom 0, battle phase 0, overview panel rect (480, 48, 160, 160), map 64x64 with an 8-byte runtime tile record, then Map_BuildLattice(0) and Map_SetZoom(0). |
| `0x0040F1A0` | `Screen_Draw(firstFrame)` | verified | The interface's master switch: 39 cases on g_screenId, each calling one screen's painter. With Screen_DrawWidgets and Screen_HandleInput these are the only three places the screen id is dispatched. docs/screens-county.md section 1. |
| `0x004B1CF3` | `Cursor_Set(kind)` | verified | The only caller of SetCursor in the binary. Maps a cursor KIND onto one of the eight HCURSORs App_InitWindow loaded: 0 and 13 -> g_cursorArrow, 2 -> g_cursorQuestion, 4 -> g_cursorCross, 5 -> g_cursorCrossTarget, 6 -> g_cursorRing, 12 -> g_cursorPeasant, 14 -> g_cursorScythe, anything else -> g_cursorArrow. Stores the choice in g_cursorCurrent. Its ONE caller is Battle_Frame, the whole-game per-frame function, which outside the battle range 0x28..0x2A just passes g_cursorByScreen[g_screenId] and inside it runs a hand-written ladder on what is under the pointer. So the pointer is re-chosen from scratch every frame and nothing else in the game touches it. docs/screens.md section 9. |
| `0x004B2258` | `App_InitWindow(inst)` | verified | The window bootstrap. Seeds the two PRNG words, sets the 640x480 canvas globals, registers window class "WinLords2Class" with hCursor NULL, LOADS THE EIGHT CURSORS with LoadCursorA from the executable's OWN resources - RT_GROUP_CURSOR 102, 103, 104, 105 (twice, into two different globals), 110, 111 and 113 - then creates the main window. hCursor NULL is why the pointer has to be set by hand: Cursor_Set runs once per frame from Battle_Frame. The twelve Cursor1..12.cur files in the install are NOT read here or anywhere: the binary imports no LoadCursorFromFile and contains no .cur filename. docs/screens.md section 9. |
| `0x004BA26E` | `Screen_DrawWidgets` | verified | Per-screen overlay pass: draws each screen's widget table through Widget_Draw and steps its animations. Same case order as Screen_Draw. |
| `0x004BA9C8` | `Screen_HandleInput` | verified | Per-screen input pass: hit-tests each screen's widget or hotspot table. Returns non-zero when a widget consumed the click. |
| `0x0040CFD2` | `Widget_Draw(xOffset, yOffset, table, count)` | verified | Draws a table of 24-byte widget records. Frame is record +0x04, plus 1 while the press timer at +0x0D runs. The size at +0x06 selects the sheet: below 24 from g_miscCtySheet, otherwise from g_systemSheet. |
| `0x0040DA1E` | `Widget_Test(xOffset, yOffset, table, count)` | verified | Hit-tests the same table Widget_Draw draws, and runs the press timer and the auto-repeat counter. The hit box is a square of side record +0x06. Publishes record +0x10 and +0x14 into g_uiHotspotId and g_uiHotspotArg, then calls record +0x08. |
| `0x0040E3EE` | `Hotspot_Test(xOffset, yOffset, table, count)` | verified | The invisible variant: the same 24-byte record read as {x0, y0, x1, y1, callback, ...} with nothing drawn. The map sidebar's buttons are these. |
| `0x0040D1BC` | `Ui_OkButton(x, y, mode)` | verified | The 24x24 picture in a panel's bottom-right corner: g_systemSheet frame 0x33 for mode 0, 0x10 for mode 1 (the same picture with a raised bevel). NOT A TICK - decoded, both frames are a cursor arrow pointing into a small black hole: a close button whose artwork is the instruction it gives. Measured rather than described: of System.pl8's 84 frames these two are the 4th and 5th darkest, 15.3% and 11.5% near-black against a median frame's 1.2%, while the genuinely thin widgets sit at 0.2% and 0.0%. It is a target as well as an instruction, and the right button closes the panel too, so the game offers two ways out: it stashes x and y into DAT_0055CE78 / DAT_0057C8A0, which FUN_0040E7E4 hit-tests on a left release - and it stashes only the LAST call's position, so a screen drawing two of them makes only the second clickable. The name is ours and predates the frame being looked at; docs/screens-county.md 2.7. |
| `0x00403DEB` | `Ui_DrawInsetRect(x, y, w, h)` | verified | A recessed rectangle: colour 0x10 along the top and right edges, 0x1F along the bottom and left. |
| `0x00402637` | `Ui_DrawText(str, x, y, font, colour)` | verified | Draws a plain string, each glyph three times - at y-1 and y+1 in two shadow colours, then at y in the real one. Advances g_penAdvance, plus four pixels of trailing space. |
| `0x00402C5E` | `Ui_DrawCentred(group, index, x, y, width, font, colour)` | verified | One L2.eng string, centred inside width. |
| `0x00402F64` | `Ui_DrawNumber(value, lead, suffix, x, y, font, colour)` | verified | A number with a leading character and a suffix. lead overwrites buffer index 0, which Ui_NumberToBuffer leaves free for a sign; '@' is the blank glyph that keeps a zero aligned with its neighbours. |
| `0x004030C6` | `Ui_DrawNumberRight(value, lead, suffix, x, y, width, font, colour)` | verified | Ui_DrawNumber, right-aligned inside width. |
| `0x00402E0C` | `Ui_DrawDelta(value, mode, prefix, suffix, x, y, font, colourPos, colourNeg)` | verified | A signed number with a prefix and a suffix - and nothing at all when the value is zero and mode is 0, which is why the happiness and population panels have blank rows in a quiet season. |
| `0x004022BD` | `Ui_NumberToBuffer(value, start, forceSign)` | verified | Formats value into g_numberBuffer starting at index start, writing '-' or '+' at start and shifting when it does. Callers pass start = 1 so index 0 stays free for a sign character. |
| `0x0041AB67` | `Ui_DrawCount(value, unitIndex, x, y, font, colour)` | verified | A number followed by a noun from L2.eng group 8: index unitIndex when the value is 1 or -1, unitIndex + 1 otherwise. Group 8 is singular at even indices and plural at odd. |
| `0x0041AC95` | `Ui_DrawHappinessDelta(value, x, y, font, colourPos, colourNeg)` | verified | Draws a bracketed signed number with the happiness face after it: a bracket, Ui_DrawDelta, g_miscCtySheet frame 0x17, a closing bracket. |
| `0x0041AC3E` | `Ui_DrawUnitNoun(value, unitIndex, x, y, font, colour)` | verified | Just the group 8 noun, singular when the value is 1. |

| `0x0042FF10` | `Screen_FrameInput()` | inferred | The FOURTH function that switches on g_screenId, and the one nothing had read: 8,140 bytes, 49 hand-written arms covering 50 screen ids, 150 g_screenId writes. Called once per frame from Battle_Frame (0x004B99C0) as the LAST thing in the frame, after every draw pass - so its navigation lands one frame late by construction. Its shape: FUN_0047685D (right-release dismisses the message scroll, and swallows the click) then Screen_HandleInput (the widget tables, LEFT BUTTON ONLY - it names no right-button global anywhere) and then, only if neither consumed the input, the per-screen arms. It is where the right mouse button lives: DAT_004E6900 (right RELEASED this frame) is tested 48 times and is how nearly every screen in the game is left, which is what L2.eng group 12 index 0, 'Click Right to Exit', tells the player. It also runs the OK-button hit test 26 times (FUN_0040E7E4), force-closes every open panel when DAT_0055403C (Turn_End) or DAT_00553FC8 (the multiplayer sync latch) says the turn is no longer the local player's, owns a falling-edge 'the map scrolled' latch across the frame boundary (DAT_0057CAC4 -> DAT_00565488), clears the WM_KEYDOWN edge DAT_004EABB4, and in individual arms sends Net_SendCommand(6, 0), calls Setup_StartGame, Map_ConfirmMoveOrder and Battle_Decline, and loads vill_gd8.pl8 and demo1.pl8. So it is not only navigation and 'HandleBack' would be far too narrow: right-close is 19 of the 49 arms and 36% of the body. INFERRED rather than verified: every line of the body has been read and the census, the state writes and the call-site placement come from the code, but the ~35 callees the arms dispatch to have not been read and the g_screenId -> screen mapping was not re-derived here. docs/screens-county.md 1 and 2.6. WHERE THE WRITES GO, from the decompilation: 100 g_screenId writes in the body (the '150' above counts the disassembly's stores, not the C statements), of which 57 are the literal 0 - the campaign map - and only 15 restore a screen the game remembered: 11 g_menuPrevScreen, 2 g_screenIdSaved, 2 g_sliderPrevScreen. So a screen's exit is a CONSTANT compiled into its own arm and not a memory of where it was opened from, which is why a panel opened over the village exits to the map and takes the village with it. The job popup is the apparent exception and is a constant too: 0x0F's arm is 'if (DAT_005533F4 == 0) g_screenId = 0x02; else g_screenId = 0', a flag the two call sites set. docs/bugs.md B63. |
| `0x0040E7E4` | `Ui_OkButtonClicked()` | verified | Hit-tests the corner picture Ui_OkButton last drew: returns 0 unless the LEFT button was released this frame (DAT_004E65D8) inside a 24x24 box at (DAT_0055CE78, DAT_0057C8A0), and on a hit calls FUN_004B18E3 to consume the click. Ui_OkButton is the only writer of those two globals and stashes only its LAST call's position, so on a screen that draws two corner pictures only the second is clickable - which is safe only because the draw pass runs before this one in the frame. Screen_FrameInput calls it 26 times. The message scroll has its own, larger hotspot: FUN_0047685D tests a 48x48 box round a copy of the same coordinates. |
| `0x0043CE1A` | `Map_Click()` | verified | The campaign map's left-click dispatcher, and the only writer of g_screenId = 2 in the whole binary. Map_PickTile then Map_ResolvePick, then: a picked unit of type 1 that is yours opens its orders or siege preparation (screen 0x1D); one of type 3 that is yours opens the merchant (screen 0x08); otherwise the tile's own flags decide - g_pickedTileFlags bit 0x80 toggles the industry the building belongs to (Industry_ToggleFromMap), bit 0x40 opens the VILLAGE (screen 0x02), bit 0x20 opens screen 0x04. A county that is not yours gets Msg_Enqueue group 0x70 instead. The village branch centres the map on the county's town tile (+0x70) and runs one Map_DrawFrame before setting the screen - which is only worth doing because the map stays visible behind the village. docs/decisions.md C22. |
| `0x0046D5FE` | `Map_ResolvePick()` | verified | Turns the picked tile into the four things Map_Click branches on: g_pickedTileCounty from the county plane, g_pickedCountyOwner from that county's +0x05, g_pickedTileFlags from the attribute plane at 0x00522F91, and g_pickedTileGraphic from g_tiles. Then masks off three flag/graphic combinations that would otherwise be hotspots. |
| `0x0043D309` | `Industry_ToggleFromMap(county, industry)` | verified | Clicking an industry building on the campaign map switches that industry on or off: it XORs the enable byte at +0x297 + industry*0x18 - the byte Labour_Allocate gates each mining job on - and maps the industry to its labour slot (0 wood -> 6, 1 iron -> 4, 2 weapons -> 7, 3 stone -> 5). Argument 4 is castle building instead, and toggles +0x1B0. Map_Click picks the argument from a ladder on g_pickedTileGraphic: 0..3 iron, 4..6 stone, 7..9 weapons, 10..12 wood, 13..20 nothing, 21+ castle. [V] The last statement is Msg_Enqueue with id 0xE6 + industry*2 + on, and L2.eng groups 228..237 are ten consecutive ONE-STRING groups that land in exactly that order: 230/231 Forestry, 232/233 Mining, 234/235 Blacksmith, 236/237 Quarrying - which confirms the industry numbering 0 wood, 1 iron, 2 weapons, 3 stone independently of the labour ladder. The castle arm does not use industry*2 at all; it writes -2 or -1 by hand, landing on 228 Building off and 229 Building on. A player described this mechanic unprompted the day it was found - 'you can click on the forest or mine on the main map to turn them off for that county. Forestry off. Forestry On.' - and the shipped Readme.txt describes it a third time. See docs/kingdom.md 7.4.1. |
| `0x00422D22` | `Screen_BattlePrompt()` | verified | Screen 0x12: the 'a battle is to be fought' prompt. Draws L2.eng group 80 heading 0, or 7 'The Siege commences.' when g_battleIsSiege; then group 80 string 1 'Will you take the field?', 2 'Your opponent has the choice' or 3 'The opponents are deciding', selected by DAT_005533D0 (who owns the choice). County name from group 100 at g_scenarioIndex*20 + the battle county, the two lords' names from g_playerNames (group 99 string 0 '"The people."' for owner 6), and both army rosters via FUN_004224E7(g_battleArmyA, g_battleArmyB). Opened by FUN_004A7158 and its sibling, which play s080_01/02/03.wav on the same DAT_005533D0 - the WAV is named after the L2.eng group. |

| `0x0041C208` | `TileInfo_Draw()` | verified | The 'what is this tile' panel. | `0x004357A6` | `Merchant_StallHit()` | verified | Screen 0x08's hit test, and the reason the merchant screen has no rectangles anywhere: good = grid[(mouseX>>3) + (mouseY>>3) * 0x50] out of mercgrid.pl8, which Screen_Merchant read whole into the village's grid buffer. 0 means the pointer is on no ware; otherwise a left press opens screen 0x0C on that good. [V] mercgrid.pl8 is 4,824 bytes = a 24-byte .pl8 header plus an 80 x 60 byte map of 8-pixel cells over the whole 640x480 screen, and the SHIPPED FILE HOLDS EXACTLY TWELVE IDS - 1,2,4,6,7,8,9,10,11,12,13,14. Sheep (3) and wool (5) are on no cell, so there is nowhere on the stall to click for either: the artwork's own statement of what Merchant_Trade's missing branches and g_goodsPrice's two zeros already said. |
| `0x0041608B` | `Merchant_HoverPlaque()` | verified | Screen 0x08's widget pass. The plaque is a 8x4-cell box, the group 6 name centred in it, and BOTH PRICES side by side at (x+0x24, y+0x22): the stall price, then stall + max(1, Pct(stall, merchant morale)), the same arithmetic as Trade_BeginGood written out a second time. It is throttled by a countdown at 0x0053EF54 that is set to 500 on entry and after every change, so the plaque does not chase a moving pointer. |

| `0x004BBC80` | `Tick_Pulses()` | verified | The animation clock for the whole interface: eight boolean pulses, all cleared at the top of every call and set again by a divider chain, so each is true only on the frame it fires. A 20 ms gate (timeGetTime() - last > 0x13) steps a counter; every 4 of those - 80 ms - sets g_pulse80 and steps six more counters, which set their own flags every 2, 4, 8, 13, 16, 24 and 32 of the 80 ms pulse: 160, 320, 640, 1040, 1280, 1920 and 2560 ms. Village_Animate takes 80 ms and 160 ms, and so does FUN_004071A0, the campaign flag. This is where any millisecond figure quoted for an animation in this project comes from. |

[V] The re-seed is why walking back to the levy screen and forward again strips the men - the slider does not do it, this does. |
| `0x00435C89` | `RaiseArmy_HireToggle` | verified | Screen 0x17, DAT_004DD340 records 1 and 2 - the tick (frame 29, hotspot id 1) at (352, 256) and the cross (frame 31, id 0) at (400, 260): DAT_0055446C = (g_uiHotspotId == 1). [V] The 'Yes'/'No' word the painter prints at (0x1D0, base + 0x98) is a READ-OUT of the flag, 32 pixels clear of the cross, and nothing tests it. |
| `0x00418FC5` | `Armoury_RestoreWalkerStrip` | verified | Screen 0x0A and 0x0D, per frame: restores whichever of the four background strips Screen_Armoury saved (y 0xD8, height 0x9E; x 0, 0xA0, 0x140 at width 0x3C and x 0x1E0 at width 0x28) according to the walking soldier's own x, DAT_0056D630. [V] Was Armoury_DrawPanel in docs/hypotheses.json, which had the role right and the verb wrong: it erases, it does not draw. Promoted with Armoury_DrawWalker and Armoury_DrawTorches, which are the other two thirds of the 0x0A overlay pass. |
| `0x004190DB` | `Armoury_DrawWalker` | verified | Screen 0x0A and 0x0D, per frame: advances the soldier who walks across the armoury after a rack is equipped - x += 4 from -0x50 along y = 0xD8, out of the trp_<weapon>_<colour>.pl8 buffer FUN_004AABD8 loaded, stopping the walk at x = 0x280. The frame cycles 0..7 until he reaches DAT_00568228, the per-type x from the table at 0x004DE6F0, and then runs a second cycle from frame 8. [V] Presentation only; nothing here reads or writes the levy basket. |
| `0x00419243` | `Armoury_DrawTorches` | verified | Screen 0x0A and 0x0D, per frame: two guttering torches from armtorch.pl8 at (0x9A, 0x76) and (0x19A, 0x76), the second thirteen frames behind the first. [V] Presentation only. |

**Globals**

| Address | Name | Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004E65FC` | `g_mouseX` | verified | Pointer x in 640x480 canvas pixels, scaled from the window by the frame poll at 0x004B2D5A. |
| `0x004E65F4` | `g_mouseY` | verified | Pointer y in canvas pixels. |
| `0x004E65CC` | `g_mouseLeftDown` | verified | LEVEL, not an edge: the left button is down right now. (DAT_004EABC2 & 1), which the window procedure sets on WM_LBUTTONDOWN (0x201) and clears on WM_LBUTTONUP (0x202). This is the flag a drag reads - the split slider tracks on it. |
| `0x004EAFB4` | `g_mouseLeftPressed` | verified | The left button went DOWN this frame - the down edge of g_mouseLeftDown. |
| `0x004E65D8` | `g_mouseLeftReleased` | verified | The left button came UP this frame. Read wherever a click is acted on, which is most of the interface. |
| `0x004E8780` | `g_mouseRightDown` | verified | LEVEL: the right button is down. (DAT_004EABC2 & 2). |
| `0x004EABE0` | `g_mouseRightPressed` | verified | The right button went down this frame. Read five times in the binary and never to dismiss anything. |
| `0x004E6900` | `g_mouseRightReleased` | verified | The right button came up this frame. This is the one every 'click right to exit' arm reads. |
| `0x004EABC5` | `g_mouseLeftDoubleClick` | verified | A left DOUBLE click arrived this frame: (DAT_004EADA1 & 1), set by the window procedure on WM_LBUTTONDBLCLK (0x203). Windows counts the clicks, not the game. Read in exactly one place - Village_DoubleClick - and setting it also clears g_mouseClickPending, so the deferred single click never fires behind it. |
| `0x004EA4B4` | `g_mouseRightDoubleClick` | verified | The right-button twin, from WM_RBUTTONDBLCLK (0x206). Computed every frame and never read. |
| `0x004EA4B0` | `g_mouseInputChanged` | verified | The pointer moved or a button changed state this frame. A drag reads it together with g_mouseLeftDown so that holding the button still does nothing. |
| `0x004E65E8` | `g_mouseClickPending` | verified | A left release is waiting to find out whether it is half of a double click. Armed on release when g_mouseClickArm is set, with the position stashed in g_mouseClickX/Y; cleared by g_mouseLeftDoubleClick, or after 300 ms by setting g_mouseClickSettled. |
| `0x004EABF0` | `g_mouseClickSettled` | verified | The pending left click stood for more than 300 ms without a second one, so it really was a single click. Village_ClickJob - the job popup - is gated on this and on nothing else, which is why that popup opens a fraction after the button comes up. |
| `0x004E65F0` | `g_mouseClickArm` | verified | Set by the screen-0x02 arm of Screen_FrameInput at the end of a frame it did not consume, and it is what makes the NEXT release arm g_mouseClickPending. So the deferred click exists on the village and nowhere else. |
| `0x004EAC04` | `g_mouseClickX` | verified | Where the pending click was released. Village_ClickJob reads this rather than the live pointer, so the popup opens on the cluster the button came up over. |
| `0x004EAC08` | `g_mouseClickY` | verified | The y of the same. |
| `0x0053EF54` | `g_merchantHoverDamper` | verified | The merchant stall's hover throttle, and THIS is the damper a tooltip needs. Screen_Merchant sets it to 500 on entry; Merchant_HoverPlaque decrements it once per widget pass and swaps the price plaque only at zero, then sets it back to 500. So the plaque does not chase a moving pointer. It is screen 0x08's alone - there is no generic tooltip mechanism in this engine, and the merchant's mouseover is one function with one counter. |
| `0x0057CB18` | `g_mapZoom` | verified | Campaign map zoom: 0 near (58x30 tiles) or 2 far (10x6). Written only by Map_SetZoom and Map_InitMode, and no caller ever passes 1, so the middle zoom is unreachable. |
| `0x0055CD48` | `g_mapScrollStep` | verified | Lattice columns per scroll step: 1 near, 4 far. Rows move by twice this, which keeps the origins parity. |
| `0x0053E8AC` | `g_mapViewCols` | verified | Lattice columns visible: 8 near, 40 far. The lattice is 65 wide, so neither zoom shows the whole map. |
| `0x0056D67C` | `g_mapViewRows` | verified | Lattice rows visible: 30 near, 128 far. Map_RenderIso draws this many plus one, the first and last half-height. |
| `0x0052AFDC` | `g_mapViewX` | verified | Left edge of the map viewport in screen pixels: 0 at both live zooms (4 at the dead middle one). |
| `0x00553244` | `g_mapViewY` | verified | Y of the first drawn lattice row: 9 near, 21 far. That row is top-clipped, so the visible band starts at viewY + rowStep = 24 at both zooms. |
| `0x00568220` | `g_mapTilePitch` | verified | Screen pixels between lattice columns: 60 near, 12 far - the tile frame width plus two. |
| `0x0057C97C` | `g_mapHalfPitch` | verified | Half the tile pitch: 30 near, 6 far. |
| `0x00567950` | `g_mapTileHalfStep` | verified | The same half pitch again, used as the x step for the half-offset lattice rows and as the divisor in Map_PickTile. |
| `0x0053F65C` | `g_mapRowStep` | verified | Screen pixels between lattice rows: 15 near, 3 far - half the tile frame height. |
| `0x0055CD60` | `g_mapViewRight` | verified | g_mapTilePitch * g_mapViewCols + g_mapViewX. 480 at all three zooms, which is what leaves 160 pixels for the right column. |

| `0x005533A4` | `g_mapScrollDir` | verified | Scroll direction 0..7 clockwise from north, 8 for none. |
| `0x0053F234` | `g_optScrollSpeed` | verified | The Scroll Speed option, 0..100 in steps of ten, shown on the slider as 0..10. Map_ScrollThrottle turns it into a minimum interval of ((100-speed)/10)*12+2 ms; 0 disables scrolling. [V] The shipped default is 60 - 50 ms, 20 tiles a second - written by Options_SetDefaults. It sits at +0x54 of the persisted settings block g_options, and Menu_ScrollSpeed forms the pointer as g_options+0x54 rather than pushing the address, so a byte scan for readers misses the menu. Exactly two other references in .text: the default, and the sub inside the throttle. docs/decisions.md C59. |
| `0x0053F1E0` | `g_options` | verified | The persisted settings block: 0x468 bytes, fread/fwritten whole. | `0x0053F204` | `g_optionsMagic` | verified | 0x7EC (2028), the settings-block version. 0x004AE2C3 compares against it and re-runs Options_SetDefaults when it does not match, which is how a settings file from another build is discarded. |
| `0x004E5B14` | `g_lastScrollTick` | verified | Map_ScrollThrottle's last-accepted timestamp, from timeGetTime. Written only when a scroll step is accepted, and the excess elapsed time is discarded rather than carried - so the scroll rate quantises to the frame rate whenever a frame is longer than the interval. |

Row 0 is the unowned shading, i.e. the raster's own indices unchanged. |

## Sound

DirectSound plus the Windows multimedia API, in two halves that never meet. **Short effects
and voices** are whole files in one-shot buffers — `Sound_LoadBank` preloads a bank of them
and `Sound_PlayFile` opens one on demand. **Music** is *streamed*: `Music_OpenStream` fills a
sliced buffer and `timeSetEvent` drives `Music_Refill` behind the play cursor, which is how a
1996 game plays a 3.8 MB track without holding it.

Three option flags gate everything and there are no volumes: `g_optMusic`, `g_optSoundEffects`
and `g_optSpeech`, from screen `0x42`.

The two pickers are the interesting functions. **`Music_StartCampaign` is a progress bar** —
which of `Scroll1‑5` plays is a ladder on the share of the map the local player holds, so the
music tells you how you are doing. **`Music_StartBattle` alternates within a pair** rather
than choosing, with a separate toggle for sieges. Both are reached from `Opt_ToggleMusic`
keyed on `g_battlePhase`, which is what identifies them. `docs/mechanics.md` has the numbers.

The sample banks are the trap: the game loads **two** banks into the same cache, twelve
entries for the campaign and seventeen for a battle, so a bare slot number means two different
sounds depending on the screen.

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: sound -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x00425B10` | `Sound_Init` | verified | DirectSoundCreate(0, &g_directSound, 0), SetCooperativeLevel(hwnd, PRIORITY), then the primary buffer. Called once at start-up, immediately before the prime-and-stop of setup.wav and the pumkin measurement. |
| `0x00425D35` | `Sound_Shutdown` | verified | Stops both streaming channels and releases. |
| `0x00425E4B` | `Sound_LoadBank(names, count)` | verified | Preloads up to 20 short samples into DirectSound buffers from a char[count][16] table of file names: mmioOpenA, CreateSoundBuffer, Lock/copy/Unlock. Called twice with two different banks - see Sound_LoadKingdomBank and Sound_LoadBattleBank - so the SAME SLOT NUMBER means a different sound on the campaign than in a battle. Stores at &DAT_00522B00 + i*4 from i=0 while the players read from &DAT_00522AFC, so a SLOT is one MORE than the entry it names (docs/decisions.md C51). |
| `0x00426120` | `Sound_PlaySlot(slot)` | verified | Plays a preloaded bank slot ONLY IF THAT BUFFER IS NOT ALREADY PLAYING - it reads GetStatus and returns 0 on DSBSTATUS_PLAYING. That drop-if-busy is the whole of the original effect mixing policy: Unit_MoveInFacing asks for a movement sound on every step of every moving unit and gets one continuous march rather than a stack. SLOT NUMBERS ARE 1-BASED: this reads &DAT_00522AFC + slot*4 while Sound_LoadBank writes &DAT_00522B00 + i*4, so slot n is bank entry n-1 (docs/decisions.md C51). Gated on g_optSoundEffects. |
| `0x00426216` | `Sound_RestartSlot(slot)` | verified | SetCurrentPosition(0) then Play, unconditionally - the other verb, and the one that does NOT drop when busy. Widget_Test (0x0040DA1E) uses it for slot 1, click3.wav, on every widget press. Slot numbers are 1-based; see Sound_PlaySlot. |
| `0x004262EB` | `Sound_FreeSlot(slot)` | verified | Stop and Release one bank buffer. |
| `0x00426360` | `Sound_RewindSlot(slot)` | verified | SetCurrentPosition(0) on one bank buffer. |
| `0x004263AD` | `Music_Play(name, channel, loop)` | verified | Start a STREAMED track on a channel: channel 0 is music and is gated on g_optMusic, channel 1 is a looping effect and is gated on g_optSoundEffects. Stops whatever the channel held, copies the 16-byte name into g_musicChannelName, opens the stream and starts the refill timer. Both music pickers end here. |
| `0x004264AC` | `Music_IsActive(channel)` | verified | Whether a streaming channel is playing, with the same two option gates in front of it. |
| `0x00426534` | `Music_OpenStream(channel, loop)` | verified | Opens the RIFF through the mmio helpers and sizes a DirectSound buffer to a fixed number of slices. The streaming half of Music_Play. |
| `0x00426B92` | `Music_StartRefill(channel)` | verified | Play(LOOPING) then timeBeginPeriod + timeSetEvent(Music_Refill, channel) - a multimedia timer drives double-buffered refill. This is why the original can stream 3.8 MB tracks in 1996. |
| `0x00426D11` | `Music_Stop(channel)` | verified | timeKillEvent, timeEndPeriod, Stop, close the mmio handle. |
| `0x00426DD5` | `Music_Refill` | verified | The timeSetEvent callback that refills the streaming buffer behind the play cursor. |
| `0x00427990` | `Sound_PlayFile(name, isSpeech, loop)` | verified | One-shot: loads a whole .wav into a DirectSound buffer and plays it. isSpeech = 1 gates on g_optSpeech, 0 gates on g_optSoundEffects. Every fanfare, every troop cry and every voice line goes through here; Msg_PlayVoice is a name lookup in front of it. |
| `0x00427C9B` | `Sound_OneShotBusy` | verified | GetStatus bit test on the one-shot buffer - Sound_PlayFile will not interrupt itself. |
| `0x00427D19` | `Sound_StopOneShot` | verified | Stop and release the one-shot buffer. Msg_PlayVoice calls it before every voice line, so a lord cuts off the previous lord. |
| `0x00477B2F` | `Music_StartBattle` | verified | THE BATTLE MUSIC PICKER. Alternates within a pair rather than choosing: a field battle flips g_battleTrackField between battle1 and battle2, a siege flips g_battleTrackSiege between battle3 and battle4, and DAT_0057A0F0 selects battle4/battle5. The counter is stepped BEFORE use from zero, so the first battle of a session plays the second track of its pair. Reached from Opt_ToggleMusic when g_battlePhase is 2. Its opening branch tests whether battle2.wav EXISTS and is dead on a complete install. |
| `0x00499ACA` | `Music_StartCampaign` | verified | THE CAMPAIGN MUSIC PICKER, and it is a progress bar. countyCount < 2 -> scroll1; then shareOfMapPct < 8 -> scroll1, < 15 -> scroll2, < 29 -> scroll3, < 43 -> scroll4, else scroll5. shareOfMapPct is PctOf(countyCount, g_countyCount) - counties over counties, armies are not in it. Reached from Opt_ToggleMusic when g_battlePhase is 0, which is the WHOLE management surface: there is no separate county track. Its opening branch tests whether scroll2.wav EXISTS and is dead on a complete install. |
| `0x00499CB1` | `Sound_PlayTroopCry(class)` | verified | Indexes g_troopSounds as unit*0x100 + class*0x40 + take*0x10, where unit is DAT_0055408C and take round-robins 0..3 in g_troopCryCounter per (unit, class). Class 3 forces take 0, so three cells per block are unreachable (docs/bugs.md D34). |
| `0x00499D7D` | `Sound_LoadKingdomBank` | verified | Sound_LoadBank(g_soundBankKingdom, 12) - the campaign and county effects. |
| `0x00499D97` | `Sound_LoadBattleBank` | verified | Sound_LoadBank(g_soundBankBattle, 17) - the battle effects. |
| `0x004AEF7E` | `Install_MeasurePumkin` | verified | The full-install check. Opens swor_p2.wav as a sentinel, then pumkin.wav or pumkin2.wav, calls __filelength and tests it against 151,000,000. The 320 MB is there to be WEIGHED, never played. DAT_005C9A74 is set to 1 before the test and 1 again in both success branches, so the check is inert (docs/bugs.md D31). |
| `0x004AF841` | `File_Exists(name)` | verified | Open, close, and on failure chdir to the install and retry - returns 0 missing, 1 found here, 2 found after the chdir. Not a sound function, but both music pickers open with it and reading it as "is this playing" inverts both ladders. |

## Diplomacy, messages and the AI lords

The four AI lords, the per-pair standing between realms, the seven letters a player can send,
and the message window that shows the replies. Written up in full in
[`diplomacy.md`](diplomacy.md).

`docs/mechanics.md` carried diplomacy as a one-line gap until a player mentioned in passing
that you can message the nobles, ally with them and send them gold. All of it is here, and so
is the answer to *"the bishop always builds really big castles"*: lord 4 has both the cheapest
royal-castle threshold and the largest free-gold row, and it is the same byte in both tables.

A message **is** an `L2.eng` group id, and for the 28 groups 170 … 197 the variant index is
`lord × 4 + rot − 4` — which is also the index into a table of 448 shipped `.wav` filenames
prefixed `Kt`, `Bn`, `Ct`, `Bp`, one set per lord.

<!-- BEGIN symbols.json: diplomacy -->

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004A1C53` | `Diplo_Init()` | verified | Clears every inbox and every realm-pair record at new game. The one place each field of the 16-byte pair record at realm +0x84 + other*0x10 is written, which is what makes the field map in docs/diplomacy.md a reading rather than a guess: standing = 5 for an in-play AI realm and 0 otherwise, allied/grudge/warningsSent/atWar/complimentsFrom/hasMail = 0, bestGift = 0, helpPriceMultiple = 1. |
| `0x004A2621` | `Diplo_Post(from, to, kind, gold, county)` | verified | The only writer of g_diploInbox. Fills the first free of five slots; bumps the recipient pair record's complimentsFrom when kind == 1; sets hasMail; and moves the gold immediately, clamped to what the sender holds - a gift is spent when it is posted, not when it is answered. |
| `0x004A277D` | `Diplo_AnswerInbox(realm)` | verified | AI turn step 1. Walks the realm's five inbox slots, dispatching kind 0..6 to the seven reply handlers, then zeroes all five slots and all six hasMail bytes. An inbox is emptied every turn whether or not it was full, and a human realm never runs this because the AI turn machine is skipped for it. |
| `0x004A2584` | `Diplo_ClearInboxes()` | verified | Zeroes all 6 x 5 inbox slots. Called by Diplo_Init. |
| `0x004A29B2` | `Diplo_ReplyGift(me, them, gold)` | inferred | Kind 0. Three tiers against best + T/2 and best + T, where T = personality +0x08 and best = pair.bestGift: L2.eng group 173 and standing -8, group 172 and +5, group 171 and +10. Then bestGift = max(bestGift, gold), so the bar ratchets and each gift must beat the last. |
| `0x004A2CD6` | `Diplo_ReplyCompliment(me, them)` | inferred | Kind 1. On pair.complimentsFrom, which Diplo_Post incremented and nothing ever resets: 1 -> group 174 and standing +15, 2 -> group 175 and +8, 3 or more -> group 176 and -4. Group 176 is 'You are boring me now with your groveling letters.' |
| `0x004A2F61` | `Diplo_ReplyInsult(me, them)` | inferred | Kind 2. Breaks any alliance between the two, standing -20, replies with L2.eng group 177. |
| `0x004A30F6` | `Diplo_ReplyAllianceOffer(me, them)` | inferred | Kind 3. Refuses with group 196 if already at war, 197 if I already have an ally, 178 otherwise; accepts with 179 and Diplo_FormAlliance. Accepts outright at standing >= 11, refuses outright below -10, and in between rolls g_rand7B < standing*3 + 45 out of 128. Accept +4, plain refusal -2, 'cannot' refusal -1. |
| `0x004A3475` | `Diplo_ReplyAllianceEnd(me, them)` | inferred | Kind 4. Breaks the alliance and takes standing -15. Sends no message at all - terminating an alliance from the diplomacy screen is silent. Group 182 'Broken alliance.' comes from Diplo_Offend instead, when an act rather than a letter breaks one. |
| `0x004A3551` | `Diplo_ReplyHelpRequest(me, them, county)` | inferred | Kind 5. Refuses with group 183 (+1 grudge) unless they are my ally, my gold is at least personality +0x30, and standing is at least 10; then g_rand7B < standing*4 accepts with group 184 and marches for free, otherwise group 185 demands personality[+0x0C] * pair.helpPriceMultiple gold. |
| `0x004A38DB` | `Diplo_ReplyAttackRequest(me, them, county)` | inferred | Kind 6, identical in shape to Diplo_ReplyHelpRequest but with groups 186/187/188, a +2 grudge on refusal and half the acceptance odds (g_rand7B < standing*2). |
| `0x004A1774` | `Diplo_FormAlliance(a, b)` | verified | Sets pair.allied = 1 and pair.grudge = 0 both ways and realm +0x81 on both sides. Six bytes; that is the whole of what an alliance is. realm +0x81 is a single byte, so a realm has at most one ally. |
| `0x004A1A54` | `Diplo_BreakAlliance(a, b)` | verified | Clears pair.allied and realm +0x81 on whichever sides point at the other. Does not touch standing - the caller applies that. |
| `0x004A1847` | `Diplo_ReconcileAlliances()` | inferred | Called from Turn_Tick. Rebuilds the allied matrix from the realms' ally bytes and drops any pairing that is one-sided or whose partner has been eliminated. |
| `0x004A16F7` | `Diplo_ActionAllowed(actor, target)` | inferred | Returns 0 when target is actor's ally - adding 1 to the ally's grudge instead - or when the two are the same realm; 1 otherwise. Gates the offence hook, which is why hostile acts against an ally cost grudge rather than standing. |
| `0x004A1B29` | `Diplo_PayForHelp(ally, payer, county, price)` | inferred | Moves price gold from payer to ally if they can afford it, increments pair.helpPriceMultiple so the next purchase costs more, takes standing -4, and sets the ally's warTarget (realm +0xE8) to the county. Called with price 0 when the ally agreed for free, and from the pay prompt otherwise. |
| `0x004A1EE1` | `Diplo_Offend(offended, offender, amount)` | inferred | The single hook every relationship-damaging act goes through. If the offender was an ally: break it, set pair.atWar unless the offended lord is 4 (the Bishop), set warTarget, Diplo_OffendAll(offender, 15), send group 182, and add 15 to the penalty. Then standing -= amount, clamped to [-30,+30]. Once standing bottoms out at -30 against a human it walks pair.warningsSent: group 189, then 190, then 191 and war. |
| `0x004A24D1` | `Diplo_OffendAll(realm, amount)` | inferred | Lowers every other realm's standing towards this one. Only reached from Diplo_Offend when an alliance is betrayed - the one act in the game that costs reputation with third parties. |
| `0x004A1241` | `Diplo_PickAllyCandidate(realm)` | inferred | Picks the best-ranked realm that is in play, not ranked 1st, unallied, not already being courted, not at war with this one, and above standing -10. Returns 0 for none. |
| `0x004A1E6C` | `Diplo_DefaultTarget()` | inferred | Sets g_diploTarget to the first in-play realm that is not the local player, or 0. |
| `0x004A0C1D` | `AI_Diplomacy(realm)` | inferred | AI turn step 2, and the real diplomacy driver. Heals standing by +1 a turn towards every NON-HUMAN realm, so an AI's opinion of a human player never recovers. Then ages the alliance grudge and breaks the alliance past personality +0x10; then, from year 1269 and if not ranked 1st, courts Diplo_PickAllyCandidate's pick every personality +0x14 turns - silently against an AI, with group 180 against a human. Its ally-is-winning tier ladder has its comparisons inverted, so two of its three tiers are unreachable. |
| `0x004A13A6` | `AI_Taunt(realm)` | verified | AI turn step 13, and NOT "offer or break an alliance" - alliances are step 2. Only a realm ranked first taunts. Stage 0: above 39 percent of the map, count to 8, then send group 193 to every live human realm, reset and go to stage 1. Stage 1: above 27 percent, and only if the last-placed realm is human and not this realm ally, count to 8, then send group 192 to it and return to stage 0. Every letter advances voiceRotation with the 3-to-0 wrap and carries variant lord*4 + rotation - 4. The share test only gates the count, so slipping below the threshold pauses the timer rather than resetting it. |
| `0x0049EDC7` | `AI_BuildCastles(realm)` | inferred | AI turn step 6. For each owned, castle-less county above personality +0xC8 population, and while the realm has fewer than personality +0x90 builds in progress, orders the largest castle type whose gold threshold at personality +0xCC..+0xDC the treasury clears. A zero threshold means that type is not offered to that lord. The Bishop reaches the royal castle at 2,000 gold where the Knight needs 10,000, and the Baron and Countess never build one. |
| `0x0049FCC5` | `AI_EmergencyWeapons(realm)` | inferred | An AI down to one county before year 1273 is handed 100 each of weapon types 3 and 4 if its lord is 4 (the Bishop), or 100 of weapon type 0 if its lord is 3 (the Countess). The Knight and the Baron get nothing. Returns 1 if it granted anything. |

The group is an L2.eng group id and the variant selects the string within it (drawn as variant + 1, past the group's label at index 0). [D] The two dairy rungs sit above the two store rungs and, because the store counts the herd twice, the Triple dairy rung can never change an answer and the Double one can only lower it - a cattle county is fed Double where a grain county with the same food is fed Triple. For each of wood, iron and stone: a non-zero want at realm +0x70/+0x74/+0x7C buys that much, and a zero want sells everything above the lord personality reserve at +0x84/+0x88/+0x8C. Then, if gold clears personality +0x78, buys personality +0x7C of the weapon the county is making - the county weapon type 0..5 maps to market good 12/11/13/9/10/14. |

**Globals**

| Address | Name | Confidence | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x0053F0F0` | `g_diploInbox` | verified | Six realms x five message slots x 8 bytes. Slot: +0x00 sender realm (0 terminates), +0x01 kind 0..6, +0x02 a county id for kinds 5 and 6, +0x04 gold for kind 0. Written only by Diplo_Post and drained by Diplo_AnswerInbox at AI step 1, so a message takes one turn to be answered. |
| `0x00568480` | `g_messageQueue` | verified | The message ring: 50 records of 0x18 bytes. +0x00 to, +0x04 from, +0x08 L2.eng group, +0x0C variant, +0x11 category (window layout), +0x12 county, +0x14 payload. Head g_messageQueueHead, tail g_messageQueueTail, both wrapping at 50. |

| `0x00553EC4` | `g_messageQueueTail` | inferred | Read index into g_messageQueue, wrapping at 50. Msg_Pump advances it. |
| `0x00553510` | `g_messageCompose` | verified | The 0x18-byte staging record Msg_Enqueue fills before copying it into g_messageQueue. |
| `0x0053E910` | `g_messageGroup` | inferred | The L2.eng group of the message currently on screen; 0 means none. |
| `0x005440AC` | `g_messageVariant` | inferred | The variant index of the message on screen. Drawn as string variant + 1 and passed straight to Msg_PlayVoice. |
| `0x005440A4` | `g_messageCategory` | inferred | The category byte of the message on screen; selects the window layout in Msg_DrawWindow and the timing rules in Msg_Pump. |

| `0x00565484` | `g_messagePending` | inferred | Set by Msg_Enqueue when something has been queued, cleared by Msg_Reset. |
| `0x004E0458` | `g_msgVoiceLord` | verified | 16-byte .wav filenames for the 28 diplomatic message groups 170..197, indexed (group - 170) * 0x100 + variant * 0x10. Sixteen per group: Kt<g>_1..4, Bn<g>_1..4, Ct<g>_1..4, Bp<g>_1..4 - Knight, Baron, Countess, Bishop, four takes each. 0x004E0458 + 28 * 0x100 = 0x004E2058, where the next filename table begins, so the extent is exact and all 448 files are present in the install. |
| `0x004DF9B8` | `g_msgVoice100` | verified | 16-byte .wav filenames S100_01 .. S169_01, one per L2.eng group 100..169, indexed (group - 100) * 0x10. Seventy entries, ending exactly at g_msgVoice200. |

| `0x0057A0F8` | `g_diploGold` | inferred | The gold amount typed on the gift page; forced to 0 for every kind other than 0. |
| `0x00553F38` | `g_diploMenuState` | inferred | Which of the four group 72 menu layouts the diplomacy screen draws: 0 no ally, 1 allied to this realm, 2 allied to another, 3 a message from this realm is already waiting. |
| `0x0053F2B8` | `g_diploLetterDraft` | inferred | Four 200-byte free-text letter buffers, one per message kind 1..4, typed by the player on screen 0x1A. |
| `0x00567D10` | `g_diploLetter` | inferred | One 0xCA-byte outgoing letter per realm. Diplo_SendClicked copies the draft here and Msg_DrawWindow draws it for the recipient; it is saved and loaded with the game. |
| `0x00567958` | `g_diploHelpPrice` | inferred | The gold an ally is demanding for help or an attack: personality +0x0C times the pair record's helpPriceMultiple, so it rises with every purchase. |
| `0x0055CE50` | `g_diploHelpCounty` | inferred | The county the pay-for-help prompt refers to. |
| `0x005530B0` | `g_pickedCounty` | inferred | The county last picked on the minimap; the diplomacy screen reads it for message kinds 5 and 6. |
| `0x00553D24` | `g_rankLeader` | verified | The top-ranked realm: the first entry of g_rankTable that Score_RankRealms did not zero out. [V] With g_rankTrailer, the last such entry, it is the victory condition - leader == trailer means one realm is left standing, since both are realm indices rather than scores. An AI also envies an ally that holds this slot. Both are 0 when nobody is in play, which passes the same test and makes the original crown g_realms[0]. |
| `0x00522D90` | `g_rankTrailer` | verified | The bottom-ranked realm: the last entry of g_rankTable that Score_RankRealms did not zero out. [V] See g_rankLeader - the two being equal is the game's victory condition, and it compares realm indices, not scores. |
| `0x00554004` | `g_realmsActive` | inferred | A realm counter with TWO writers and two meanings: FUN_0049B6D3 sets it to the number of in-play realms and Turn_AllRealmsDone to the number that have not yet finished their turn. The diplomacy code tests it against 3 and reads whichever wrote last. Both readings mean 'the game is nearly over' and the branch refuses either way, but which was intended is not established. |
| `0x0058FD60` | `g_rand7B` | verified | randStateB & 0x7F, so 0..127. The die the alliance and help/attack decisions roll. |
| `0x004DC17C` | `g_lordChoice` | verified | Which of the four lords each colour slot may be given at new game: 4 scenario groups x 5 colour slots x 4 candidate lord ids, indexed 0x004DC17C + (g_scenarioIndex & 3) * 0x14 + realm[+0x0A] * 4 + n. Setup walks the four candidates and takes the first lord not already used, so THE LORD IS NOT THE REALM INDEX AND NOT THE COLOUR - every row except one is a permutation of 1..4, and 4 x 0x14 = 80 bytes ends at 0x004DC1CC. g_aiPersonality and g_aiGoldGrant are both indexed by realm +0x07, the lord, not by the realm slot. |
| `0x004DC1CE` | `g_realmColour` | inferred | Two palette bytes per colour slot, indexed by realm +0x0A (1..5), copied into realm +0x08 and +0x09 at new game: (14,15) (251,13) (58,32) (5,253) (4,240). The colour slot is handed out in realm order and is independent of which lord the realm gets. |
| `0x004DD940` | `g_diploWidgets` | verified | The diplomacy screen's widgets: six at (400, 102 + 50n) opening L2.eng group 72 rows 2..8 in order, then a plus/minus pair at (184, 240) and (216, 240) for the gift amount. |
| `0x00553EE8` | `g_diploWidgetCount` | verified | How many of g_diploWidgets the current group 72 menu layout uses. |
| `0x00553EE0` | `g_messageFrom` | verified | The `from` field of the message being displayed, taken out of the queue record by Msg_Pump. [V] The ending logic compares it against g_localPlayer: an elimination notice whose sender is me is a loss, and one about somebody else is not. It is also the realm whose player name the message window draws, except for group 225, which draws the local player's. |

## Networking

| Address | Name | Confidence | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x004B7FCE` | call to `DirectPlayEnumerateA` | verified | The only call site. Populates the connection-type list. |
| `0x004B826E` | call to `DirectPlayCreate` | verified | The only call site. |

These two are call instructions, not function entry points, which is why they are not in
`symbols.json`.

The exe imports exactly two functions from `DPLAYX.dll`, by ordinals 1 and 2 — the whole
network entry surface. It also references `sierranw.dll` and `snwvalid.dll` (Sierra's
online matchmaking), but neither ships with the GOG release and neither appears in the
import table.

**That path is reached, not dead.** An earlier revision here called it dead code. In fact
the multiplayer menu reaches it and fails with a modal: *"SNWValid.dll not found in Windows
system folder."* The DLL exists nowhere - neither system folder, nor the game directory.
So the GOG release cannot do its original multiplayer at all, and any attempt to capture a
live DirectPlay session has to get past that check first. See `docs/netcode.md`.

## The game logs its own startup

`Lords2.exe` writes `status.txt` beside itself, narrating initialisation with lines like
`OK :DD Set resolution.` and `ERR:`-prefixed failures. This is the best available anchor
for identifying subsystems: find a log string, find what writes it, and a subsystem is
named.

It also looks for a `sierra.ini` that the GOG release doesn't ship, and logs the failure.
Not known to be fatal.

## Layout

```
.text   0x401000 – 0x4CFB06   ~846 KB of code
.rdata  0x4D0000
.data   0x4D2000             ~1 MB virtual, only 80 KB on disk
                             → ~955 KB of zero-initialised globals: the game state
.idata  0x5CF000
```

That `.data` region is why incremental replacement works: the live game state sits at
fixed addresses and can be read from another process while the game runs.
